\displaystyle=k\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\left[\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\mathcal{A}}\nabla_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}}\theta+\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}}\nabla_{\mathcal{A}}\theta\right]\cdot wJ+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}F^{8}wJ

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Sigma}(k\nabla_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}}\theta\cdot\mathcal{N}+k\nabla_{\mathcal{A}}\theta\cdot\partial_{t}\mathcal{N}+\theta\partial_{t}|\mathcal{N}|-\partial_{t}F^{9}|)w

\displaystyle-(\partial_{t}JK(\partial_{t}\theta-k\Delta_{\mathcal{A}}\theta-F^{8}),w)_{\mathcal{H}^{0}_{T}}.

Since
\theta
is a strong solution to (
1.19
), we then plug the equations of (
1.19
) into the last line of (
3.42
) to cancel the terms of
\partial_{t}J
to arrive that the weak solution to (
3.18
) follows from (
3.19
) in the sense of (
3.19
).

∎

3.4.
Higher Regularity

In order to state our higher regularity results for the problem (
1.20
), we must be able to bound the forcing term that results from temporally differentiating (
1.20
) one time. We consider the system with
j=1,2
:

(3.43)

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{t}^{j+1}\theta-k\Delta_{\mathcal{A}}\partial_{t}^{j}\theta=F^{8,j}\quad&\text{in}&\quad\Omega,\\
&k\mathcal{N}\cdot\nabla_{\mathcal{A}}\partial_{t}^{j}\theta+\partial_{t}^{j}\theta|\mathcal{N}|=F^{9,j}\quad&\text{on}&\quad\Sigma,\\
&\partial_{t}^{j}\theta=0\quad&\text{on}&\quad\Sigma_{s},\end{aligned}\right.

Now, with the forcing terms of
F^{i,1}
defined in (
B.4
), we have the following estimate .

Lemma 3.10
.

The following estimates hold.

(3.44)

\displaystyle\|F^{8,1}\|_{L^{2}L^{q_{-}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial_{t}F^{8}\|_{L^{2}L^{q_{-}}}^{2}+\mathfrak{E}(\eta)(\|\theta\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{-}}}^{2}+\|\theta\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}^{2})

\displaystyle+\mathfrak{D}(\eta)(\|\theta_{0}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\theta\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{-}}}\|\partial_{t}\theta\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}),

(3.45)

\displaystyle\|F^{9,1}\|_{L^{2}W^{1-1/q_{-},q_{-}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial_{t}F^{9}\|_{L^{2}W^{1-1/q_{-},q_{-}}}^{2}+\mathfrak{E}(\eta)\|\theta\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{-}}}^{2}.

Proof.

According to the definition of
F^{8,1}
and
F^{9,1}
in (
B.4
), we use Leibniz rule to rewrite
F^{i,1}
as a sum of products for two terms. One term is a product of various derivatives of
\bar{\eta}
, and the other is linear for derivatives of
\theta
. Then for a.e.
t\in[0,T]
, we estimate these resulting products using the product estimates in
[
11
]
, the Sobolev embedding and trace theory, the smallness of
\mathfrak{K}(\eta)
and the finite volume of
|\Omega|
. Then the resulting inequality after integrating over
[0,T]
reveals

\displaystyle\|F^{8,1}\|_{L^{2}L^{q_{-}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial_{t}F^{8}\|_{L^{2}L^{q_{-}}}+\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{L^{\infty}H^{3/2+(\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha)/2}}(1+\|\eta\|_{L^{\infty}W^{1-1/q_{+},q_{+}}})\|\theta\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{-}}}

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{L^{2}W^{3-1/q_{-},q_{-}}}\|\theta\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1+\varepsilon_{-}/2}}.

Then we use the Sobolev extension and restriction theory to have that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\|\theta\|_{H^{1+\varepsilon_{-}/2}}^{2}\leq\|\theta\|_{H^{1+\varepsilon_{-}}}\|\partial_{t}\theta\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim\|\theta\|_{W^{2,q_{-}}}\|\partial_{t}\theta\|_{H^{1}},

which, after an integration by parts, implies

\|\theta\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1+\varepsilon_{-}/2}}^{2}\lesssim\|\theta_{0}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\theta\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{-}}}\|\partial_{t}\theta\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}.

Thus we have the bounds for (
3.44
). Similarly, we have the bounds for (
3.45
).
∎

We now present the dual estimates for forcing terms.

Lemma 3.11
.

The following estimates hold that

\displaystyle\|F^{8,1}+F^{9,1}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}^{1})^{\ast}}\lesssim\mathfrak{E}(\eta)(\|\theta\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{-}}}+\|\partial_{t}(F^{8}+F^{9})\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}^{1})^{\ast}},

\displaystyle\|F^{8,1}+F^{9,1}(0)\|_{(\mathcal{H}^{1})^{\ast}}\lesssim\mathfrak{E}(\eta)(0)(\|\theta_{0}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}+\|\partial_{t}(F^{8}+F^{9})(0)\|_{(\mathcal{H}^{1})^{\ast}},

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}(F^{8,1}+F^{9,1})\|_{(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{T})^{\ast}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial_{t}^{2}(F^{8}+F^{9})\|_{(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{T})^{\ast}}+\mathfrak{E}(\eta)(\|\partial_{t}\theta\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{-}}}+\|\theta\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{-}}}

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{t}F^{8}\|_{L^{2}L^{q_{-}}}+\|\partial_{t}F^{9}\|_{L^{2}W^{2-1/q_{-},q_{-}}})+\mathfrak{D}(\eta)\|\theta\|_{L^{\infty}W^{2,q_{+}}}.

Proof.

Since the proof of the first inequality is similar to Lemma
3.10
, so we only give the proof of the third inequality, which is much harder.
From the notation in Section
1.4
, we have that

(3.46)

\left<\partial_{t}(F^{8,1}+F^{9,1}),\psi\right>_{(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{T})^{\ast}}=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}F^{8,1}\psi J+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{-\ell}^{\ell}\partial_{t}F^{9,1}\psi,

for each
\psi\in\mathcal{H}^{1}_{T}
.
By the definition of
F^{i,j}
in (
B.4
), (
3.46
) is reduced to

(3.47)

\displaystyle\left<\partial_{t}(F^{8,1}+F^{9,1}),\psi\right>_{(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{T})^{\ast}}=\left<\partial_{t}^{2}(F^{8}+F^{9}),\psi\right>_{(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{T})^{\ast}}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}G^{8}\psi J+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{-\ell}^{\ell}\partial_{t}G^{9}\psi,

for each
\psi\in\mathcal{H}^{1}
, where
G^{8}
and
G^{9}
are defined as in Theorem
3.9
. It is trivial that

(3.48)

\left<\partial_{t}^{2}(F^{8}+F^{9}),\psi\right>_{(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{T})^{\ast}}\leq\|\partial_{t}^{2}(F^{8}+F^{9})\|_{(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{T})^{\ast}}\|\psi\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{T}}.

By the Hölder inequality, Sobolev embedding inequality and usual trace theory, we have

(3.49)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}G^{8}\psi J&\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\|\psi\|_{L^{2/\varepsilon_{-}}}\|\nabla^{2}\theta\|_{L^{q_{-}}}\|\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}\|_{W^{1,\infty}}^{2}\\
&\quad+\int_{0}^{T}\|\psi\|_{L^{4/(3\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha)}}\|\nabla\theta\|_{L^{2/(1-\varepsilon_{-})}}\|\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}\|_{W^{2,4/(2+\alpha-\varepsilon_{-})}}\|\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}\|_{W^{1,\infty}}\\
&\quad+\int_{0}^{T}\|\psi\|_{L^{2/(\varepsilon_{+}-2\alpha)}}\|\partial_{t}^{2}\eta\|_{W^{2,2/(1+2\alpha)}}\|\theta\|_{W^{1,2/(1-\varepsilon_{+})}}\\
&\quad+\int_{0}^{T}\|\psi\|_{L^{2/(\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha)}}\|\partial_{t}^{2}\eta\|_{W^{1,2/\alpha}}\|\nabla^{2}\theta\|_{L^{q_{-}}}\\
&\lesssim(\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{L^{\infty}H^{3/2+(\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha)/2}}\|\theta\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{-}}}+\|\theta\|_{L^{\infty}W^{2,q_{+}}}\|\partial_{t}^{2}\eta\|_{L^{2}H^{3/2-\alpha}})\|\psi\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{T}}.\end{aligned}

We now turn to estimate the integration on the boundary. By the Hölder’s inequality and Sobolev inequalities, we have

(3.50)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \int_{0}^{T}\int_{-\ell}^{\ell}\partial_{t}G^{9}\psi&\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\int_{-\ell}^{\ell}|\partial_{t}^{2}\mathcal{A}||\nabla\theta||\psi|+|\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}||\nabla\partial_{t}\theta||\psi|+|\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}||\nabla\theta||\partial_{t}\partial_{1}\eta||\psi|+|\theta||\partial_{1}\partial_{t}^{2}\eta||\psi|\\
&\quad+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{-\ell}^{\ell}|\nabla\partial_{t}\theta||\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\eta||\psi|+|\nabla\theta||\partial_{1}\partial_{t}^{2}\eta||\psi|+|\partial_{t}\eta||\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\theta||\psi|+|\theta||\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\eta||\psi|\\
&\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}(\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{W^{1,1/\alpha}}+\|\partial_{t}^{2}\eta\|_{W^{1,1/\alpha}})\|\nabla\theta\|_{L^{1/(1-\varepsilon_{+})}(\Sigma)}\|\psi\|_{L^{1/(\varepsilon_{+}-\alpha)}(\Sigma)}\\
&\quad+\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{W^{1,\infty}}\|\partial_{t}\theta\|_{W^{1,1/(1-\varepsilon_{-})}(\Sigma)}\|\psi\|_{L^{1/(\varepsilon_{-})}(\Sigma)}\\
&\quad+\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{W^{1,\infty}}^{2}\|\theta\|_{W^{1,1/(1-\varepsilon_{-})}(\Sigma)}\|\psi\|_{L^{1/(\varepsilon_{-})}(\Sigma)}\\
&\lesssim(\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{L^{2}H^{3/2-\alpha}}+\|\partial_{t}^{2}\eta\|_{L^{2}H^{3/2-\alpha}})\|\theta\|_{L^{\infty}W^{2,q_{+}}}\|\psi\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{T}}\\
&\quad+\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{L^{\infty}H^{3/2+(\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha)/2}}(\|\partial_{t}\theta\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{-}}}+\|\theta\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{-}}})\|\psi\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{T}}.\end{aligned}

Then combining (
3.48
) – (
3.50
) yields the result.
∎

Theorem 3.12
.

Assume that the forcing terms
F^{i,j}
satisfy the conditions in Lemma
3.10
and
3.11
, that
\mathfrak{K}(\eta)\leq\delta
is smaller than
\delta_{0}
in Lemma
1.3
and Theorem 4.7 in
[
11
]
.
Then there exists a unique strong solution
\theta
to (
1.19
) on temporal interval
[0,T]
. Moreover,

\partial_{t}^{j}\theta
satisfies (
3.43
)in the strong sense with initial data
\partial_{t}^{j}\theta(0)
for
j=0,1
and
\partial_{t}^{2}\theta
solves (
3.43
) in the weak sense for
j=2
. Moreover, there exists a constant
C_{0}=C_{0}(\sigma,\ell,\Omega)
, such that the solution satisfies the estimate

(3.51)

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\Big(\|\theta(t)\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\theta(t)\|_{H^{1+\varepsilon_{-}/2}}^{2}+\sum_{j=0}^{2}\|\partial_{t}^{j}\theta(t)\|_{H^{0}}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\Big(\|\theta(t)\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\theta(t)\|_{W^{2,q_{-}}}^{2}+\sum_{j=0}^{2}\|\partial_{t}^{j}\theta(t)\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\exp\{\mathfrak{E}(\eta)T\}\bigg(\|\theta_{0}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\theta(0)\|_{H^{3/2+(\varepsilon_{+}-\alpha)/2}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{2}\theta(0)\|_{H^{0}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}(F^{8}+F^{9})(0)\|_{(H^{1})^{\ast}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|F^{8}(0)\|_{L^{q_{+}}}^{2}+\|F^{9}\|(0)\|_{W^{1-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}+(1+\mathfrak{E}(\eta))\Big(\|F^{8}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{q_{+}}}^{2}+\|F^{9}\|_{L^{\infty}W^{1-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{t}F^{8}\|_{L^{2}L^{q_{-}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}F^{9}\|_{L^{2}W^{1-1/q_{-},q_{-}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{2}(F^{8}+F^{9})\|_{(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{T})^{\ast}}^{2}\Big)\bigg).

Proof.

Step 1 – Construction of iteration

Theorem
3.9
guarantees that
\partial_{t}^{j}\theta
is a solution to (
3.43
) in the strong sense when
j=0
and in the weak sense when
j=1
. For
j=1
, the assumption of Theorem
3.9
are satisfied by Lemma
3.10
, Lemma
3.11
and compatibility conditions for initial data in Section
3
. So based on the initial data convergence for Galerkin setting in Proposition
3.8
, we want to use the result in Theorem
3.9
to deduce that
\partial_{t}^{j}\theta
is a solution to (
1.19
) in the strong sense when
j=1
and in the weak sense when
j=2
.

Note that in Proposition
3.8
, the estimate for
\partial_{t}(F^{8,1}+F^{9,1})
involves
\|\partial_{t}\theta\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{-}}}
, which is our aim to be obtained in this two-tier energy method. So we cannot directly use Proposition
3.8
. In order to overcome this difficulty, we introduce an iteration:

(3.52)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{t}\vartheta^{n+1}-k\Delta_{\mathcal{A}}\vartheta^{n+1}=F^{8,1,n}\quad&\text{in}&\quad\Omega,\\
&k\mathcal{N}\cdot\nabla_{\mathcal{A}}\vartheta^{n+1}+\vartheta^{n+1}|\mathcal{N}|=F^{9,1,n}\quad&\text{on}&\quad\Sigma,\\
&\vartheta^{n+1}=0\quad&\text{on}&\quad\Sigma_{s},\end{aligned}\right.

and

(3.53)

\displaystyle\theta^{n+1}=\theta_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}\vartheta^{n+1}(s)\,\mathrm{d}s.

Here we set
F^{8,1,n}=\partial_{t}F^{8}+G^{8}(\theta^{n})
and
F^{9,1,n}=\partial_{t}F^{9}+G^{9}(\theta^{n})
. When
n=0
, we can choose
\theta^{0}=\theta_{0}
as the start point. By the Theorem
3.9
, we know that
\vartheta^{n+1}
solves (
3.52
) with initial data
\vartheta^{n+1}(0)=\partial_{t}\theta(0)
and
\partial_{t}\vartheta^{n+1}
is a weak solution with initial data
\partial_{t}\vartheta^{n+1}(0)=\partial_{t}^{2}\theta(0)
to

(3.54)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{t}(\partial_{t}\vartheta^{n+1})-k\Delta_{\mathcal{A}}\partial_{t}\vartheta^{n+1}=F^{8,2,n}\quad&\text{in}&\quad\Omega,\\
&k\mathcal{N}\cdot\nabla_{\mathcal{A}}\partial_{t}\vartheta^{n+1}+\partial_{t}\vartheta^{n+1}|\mathcal{N}|=F^{9,2,n}\quad&\text{on}&\quad\Sigma,\\
&\partial_{t}\vartheta^{n+1}=0\quad&\text{on}&\quad\Sigma_{s},\end{aligned}\right.

where
F^{8,2,n}=\partial_{t}F^{8,1,n}+k\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}}\nabla_{\mathcal{A}}\vartheta^{n+1}+k\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\mathcal{A}}\nabla_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}}\vartheta^{n+1}
, and
F^{9,2,n}=\partial_{t}F^{9,1,n}-k\partial_{t}\mathcal{N}\cdot\nabla_{\mathcal{A}}\vartheta^{n+1}-\vartheta^{n+1}\partial_{t}|\mathcal{N}|
.

We now derive the energy structure of (
3.54
) by choosing the test function
\partial_{t}\vartheta^{n+1}
such that

(3.55)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}|\partial_{t}\vartheta^{n+1}|^{2}J+k\int_{\Omega}|\nabla_{\mathcal{A}}\partial_{t}\vartheta^{n+1}|^{2}J+\int_{-\ell}^{\ell}|\partial_{t}\vartheta^{n+1}|^{2}\sqrt{|\mathcal{N}|}\\
=\left<F^{8,2,n}+F^{9,2,n},\partial_{t}\vartheta^{n+1}\right>_{\ast}-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}|\partial_{t}\vartheta^{n+1}|^{2}\partial_{t}J.\end{aligned}

By Lemma
3.10
and Lemma
3.11
, we have

(3.56)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\|\partial_{t}\vartheta^{n+1}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{0}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\vartheta^{n+1}\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\vartheta^{n+1}\|_{L^{2}H^{0}(\Sigma)}^{2}\\
&\leq C\bigg(\|\partial_{t}^{2}\theta(0)\|_{H^{0}}^{2}+\mathfrak{E}(\eta)(\|\partial_{t}F^{8}\|_{L^{2}L^{q_{-}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}F^{9}\|_{L^{2}W^{1-1/q_{-},q_{-}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\theta^{n}\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{-}}}^{2})\\
&\quad+\mathfrak{D}(\eta)\|\theta^{n}\|_{L^{\infty}W^{2,q_{-}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{2}(F^{8}+F^{9})\|_{(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{T})^{\ast}}^{2}\bigg).\end{aligned}

Furthermore, by the energy estimates and elliptic estimates to (
3.52
), we have

(3.57)

\displaystyle\|\vartheta^{n+1}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{0}}^{2}+\|\vartheta^{n+1}\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{-}}}^{2}+\|\vartheta^{n+1}\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}^{2}+\|\vartheta^{n+1}\|_{L^{2}H^{0}(\Sigma)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\bigg(\|\partial_{t}\theta(0)\|_{H^{0}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\vartheta\|_{L^{2}H^{0}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}F^{8}\|_{L^{2}L^{q_{-}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}F^{9}\|_{L^{2}W^{1-1/q_{-},q_{-}}}^{2}\bigg).

Now we have to show that the sequences
\{\vartheta^{n}\}
and
\{\theta^{n}\}
are Cauchy sequences. Set

\mathfrak{M}(\vartheta^{n+1}):=\sum_{j=0}^{1}\|\partial_{t}^{j}\vartheta^{n+1}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{0}}^{2}+\|\vartheta^{n+1}\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{-}}}^{2}+\sum_{j=0}^{1}\big(\|\partial_{t}^{j}\vartheta^{n+1}\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{j}\vartheta^{n+1}\|_{L^{2}H^{0}(\Sigma)}^{2}),

\mathfrak{N}(\theta^{n}):=\|\theta^{n}\|_{L^{\infty}W^{2,q_{-}}}^{2}+\|\theta^{n}\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{-}}}^{2}+\|\theta^{n}\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\theta^{n}\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{-}}}^{2},

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{j=1}^{2}\|\partial_{t}^{j}\theta(0)\|_{H^{0}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{2}(F^{1}-F^{4}-F^{5})\|_{(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{T})^{\ast}}^{2}+\mathfrak{D}(\eta)(\|F^{8}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{q_{+}}}^{2}+\|F^{9}\|_{L^{\infty}W^{2-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2})

\displaystyle+(1+\mathfrak{E}(\eta))(\|\partial_{t}F^{8}\|_{L^{2}L^{q_{-}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}F^{9}\|_{L^{2}W^{2-1/q_{-},q_{-}}}^{2})+\|\partial_{t}(F^{8}+F^{9})(0)\|_{(H^{1})^{\ast}}^{2}.

In the following estimates (
3.58
)– (
3.60
),
C
is a constant independent of
n
, which is allowed to be changed from line to line.
By (
3.56
) and (
3.57
), we have

(3.58)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}(\vartheta^{n+1})\leq C\mathcal{Z}+C\mathfrak{K}(\eta)\mathfrak{N}(\theta^{n}).

From (
3.53
), we get

(3.59)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{N}(\theta^{n+1})\leq CT(\|\theta_{0}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\theta_{0}\|_{H^{1}}^{2})+(1+T)\mathfrak{M}(\vartheta^{n+1}).

Then for a fixed
T>0
, due to the smallness of
\mathfrak{\eta}
, we have

(3.60)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}(\vartheta^{n+1})+\mathfrak{N}(\theta^{n+1})\leq C\mathcal{Z}

Now we show that the systems (
3.52
) coupled with (
3.53
) is a contraction mapping :
\theta^{n}\to\vartheta^{n+1}
under the topology
\mathfrak{M}
and
\mathfrak{N}
. Suppose that the mapping
\theta^{n+1}\to\vartheta^{n+2}
satisfies the systems (
3.52
) and (
3.53
), then the mapping
\theta^{n+1}-\theta^{n}\to\vartheta^{n+2}-\vartheta^{n+1}
also satisfies (
3.52
) and (
3.53
) with the forces
F^{8}=F^{9}=0
and zero initial data. Therefore the results in (
3.58
) and (
3.59
) imply

(3.61)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathfrak{M}(\vartheta^{n+2}-\vartheta^{n+1})\lesssim\mathfrak{K}(\eta)\mathfrak{N}(\theta^{n+1}-\theta^{n}).\end{aligned}

Then we use (
3.53
) to deduce that

(3.62)

\displaystyle\|\theta^{n+1}-\theta^{n}\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{-}}}^{2}\lesssim T\|\vartheta^{n+1}-\vartheta^{n}\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{-}}}^{2},\quad\|\theta^{n+1}-\theta^{n}\|_{L^{\infty}W^{2,q_{-}}}^{2}\lesssim\|\vartheta^{n+1}-\vartheta^{n}\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{-}}}^{2}.

We still use (
3.53
) to see that

(3.63)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\theta^{n+1}=\vartheta^{n+1},

so that

(3.64)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \|\partial_{t}\theta^{n+1}-\partial_{t}\theta^{n}\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{-}}}^{2}=\|\vartheta^{n+1}-\vartheta^{n}\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{-}}}^{2}.\end{aligned}

By combining all the estimates (
3.62
)–(
3.64
), we have that

(3.65)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathfrak{N}(\theta^{n+1}-\theta^{n})\lesssim(T+1)\mathfrak{K}(\eta)\mathfrak{M}(\vartheta^{n+1}-\vartheta^{n}).\end{aligned}

Hence for the restricted smallness of
\mathfrak{K}(\eta)<\gamma
, the estimates (
3.61
) and (
3.65
) show that the operator for (
3.52
) and (
3.53
) is strictly contracted. So that (
3.43
) has a unique fixed point when
j=1
. That means
\vartheta^{n}
converges to
\vartheta
in the topology of
\mathfrak{M}
, and
\theta^{n}
converges to
\tilde{\theta}
in the topology of
\mathfrak{N}
. Moreover,
\vartheta
and
\tilde{\theta}
are strong solutions to

(3.66)

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{t}\vartheta-k\Delta_{\mathcal{A}}\vartheta=F^{8,1}\quad&\text{in}&\quad\Omega,\\
&k\mathcal{N}\cdot\nabla_{\mathcal{A}}\vartheta+\vartheta|\mathcal{N}|=F^{9,1}\quad&\text{on}&\quad\Sigma,\\
&\vartheta=0\quad&\text{on}&\quad\Sigma_{s},\end{aligned}\right.

and

(3.67)

\displaystyle\tilde{\theta}=\theta_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}\vartheta(s)\,\mathrm{d}s,

with the initial data
\vartheta(0)=\partial_{t}\theta(0)
and
\tilde{\theta}(0)=\theta_{0}
.

Step 2 – Consistency of Solution

In the following, we show that
\tilde{\theta}=\theta
, which guarantees that (
3.66
) is exactly equal to the one time derivative of (
3.43
) with
j=1
.
We first use (
3.67
) and the facts that
\vartheta=\theta_{0}=0
on
\Sigma_{s}
to obtain that
\tilde{\theta}=0
on
\Sigma_{s}
.

We use (
3.67
) to get that
\vartheta=\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}
, which might be plugged into (
3.66
) so that
\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}
is a strong solution to

(3.68)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{t}(\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}-k\Delta_{\mathcal{A}}\tilde{\theta})=\partial_{t}F^{8}\quad&\text{in}\quad&\Omega,\\
&\partial_{t}(k\nabla_{\mathcal{A}}\tilde{\theta}\cdot\mathcal{N}+\tilde{\theta}|\mathcal{N}|)=\partial_{t}F^{9}\quad&\text{on}\quad&\Sigma,\\
&\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}=0\quad&\text{on}\quad&\Sigma_{s}.\end{aligned}\right.

Since the first equation in (
3.68
) holds in
L^{q_{-}}(\Omega)
, we have

(3.69)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}-k\Delta_{\mathcal{A}}\tilde{\theta}-F^{8}\|_{L^{q_{-}}}^{q_{-}}=0.

After integrating in time from
0
to
t<T
, we have

(3.70)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}-k\Delta_{\mathcal{A}}\tilde{\theta}-F^{8}\|_{L^{q_{-}}}=\|-F^{8}(0)+\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}(0)-k\Delta_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\tilde{\theta}_{0}\|_{L^{q_{-}}}.

We can still use the similar computation to deal with the boundary terms:

(3.71)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\|\nabla_{\mathcal{A}}\tilde{\theta}\cdot\mathcal{N}+\tilde{\theta}|\mathcal{N}|-F^{9}\|_{L^{q_{-}}}=\|\nabla_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\tilde{\theta}_{0}\cdot\mathcal{N}(0)+\tilde{\theta}_{0}|\mathcal{N}_{0}|)-F^{9}(0)\|_{L^{q_{-}}},\end{aligned}

and

(3.72)

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}(\Sigma_{s})}=\|\tilde{\theta}_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\Sigma_{s})}.

Thanks to the initial data of
(\theta_{0},\partial_{t}\theta(0))
satisfying (
1.19
) at
t=0
, we directly see that the initial terms in (
3.70
) – (
3.72
) all vanish. Then from the regularity of
\tilde{\theta}
, (
3.70
) – (
3.72
) implies that
\tilde{\theta}
solves (
1.19
) at least in the sense of weak solutions, since we have shown that
\theta
is also a solution to (
1.19
). Then the uniqueness of weak solutions to (
1.19
) in Proposition
3.2
guarantees that
\tilde{\theta}=\theta
. Then by the Theorem
3.9
for strong solutions to (
1.19
), we can see that
\tilde{\theta}=\theta
is the strong solution to (
1.19
). Moreover,

\mathfrak{M}(\theta)\lesssim\mathcal{Z}.

Step 3 – Estimates for energy and dissipation.

We combine the estimates following from Theorem
3.9
, the boundedness of
\mathfrak{M}(\theta)
and Lemma
3.10
, along with
\mathfrak{K}(\eta)
sufficiently small to obtain that

(3.73)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{2}\|\partial_{t}^{j}\theta\|_{L^{\infty}H^{0}}^{2}+\|\theta\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\theta\|_{L^{\infty}W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\theta\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{-}}}^{2}+\sum_{j=0}^{2}\left(\|\partial_{t}^{j}\theta\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{j}\theta\|_{L^{2}H^{0}(\Sigma)}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\exp\{\mathfrak{E}(\eta)T\}\bigg(\mathcal{E}(0)+\|F^{8}(0)\|_{L^{q_{-}}}^{2}+\|F^{9}(0)\|_{W^{1-1/q_{-},q_{-}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}(F^{8}+F^{9})(0)\|_{(H^{1})^{\ast}}^{2}

\displaystyle+(1+\mathfrak{E}(\eta))(\|\partial_{t}F^{8}\|_{L^{2}L^{q_{-}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}F^{9}\|_{L^{2}W^{1-1/q_{-},q_{-}}}^{2}+\|F^{8}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{q_{+}}}^{2}+\|F^{9}\|_{L^{\infty}W^{1-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{t}^{2}(F^{8}+F^{9})\|_{(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{T})^{\ast}}^{2})\bigg).

We now use Sobolev extension and restriction theory to estimate
\frac{d}{dt}\frac{1}{2}\|\partial_{t}\theta\|_{H^{1+\varepsilon_{-}/2}}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}\|\partial_{t}\theta\|_{1+\varepsilon_{-}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\|\partial_{t}^{2}\theta\|_{1}^{2}

so that

(3.74)

\|\partial_{t}\theta\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1+\varepsilon_{-}/2}}^{2}\lesssim\|\partial_{t}\theta(0)\|_{H^{1+\varepsilon_{-}/2}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\theta\|_{L^{2}H^{1+\varepsilon_{-}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{2}\theta\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}^{2}.

Finally, we combine (
3.73
) and (
3.74
) conclusion (
3.51
).
∎

4.
Local Well-Posedness for Nonlinear
\varepsilon
–Regularized Boussinesq System

4.1.
Solving the linear coupled system (
1.19
) and (
1.20
)

We have established the well-posedness for (
1.19
). Then we can put the
\theta
into (
1.20
) to solve the coupled linear system (
1.19
) and (
1.20
).
With
\mathcal{E}
in (
1.14
) and
\mathcal{D}
in (
1.15
), we define the quantities as follows.

\displaystyle\mathscr{D}(u,p,\xi,\theta):

\displaystyle=\|\mathcal{D}(u,p,\xi,\theta)\|_{L^{2}_{t}([0,T])}^{2}+\varepsilon\|\partial_{t}^{2}\xi\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\|\xi\|_{L^{2}W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\|\partial_{t}\xi\|_{L^{2}W^{3-1/q_{-},q_{-}}}^{2},

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}(u,p,\xi,\theta):

\displaystyle=\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathcal{E}(u,p,\xi,\theta)+\varepsilon\|\partial_{t}^{2}\xi\|_{L^{\infty}H^{3/2}}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\|\partial_{t}\xi\|_{L^{\infty}W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2},

\displaystyle\mathscr{K}(u,p,\xi,\theta):

\displaystyle=\mathscr{E}(u,p,\xi,\theta)+\mathscr{D}(u,p,\xi,\theta).

Now for convenience, we introduce two new spaces

(4.1)

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}=\Big\{(u,p,\eta,\theta)|\mathscr{E}(u,p,\eta,\theta)<\infty\Big\},\quad\|(u,p,\eta,\theta)\|_{\mathcal{X}}=\left[\mathscr{E}(u,p,\eta,\theta)\right]^{1/2},

\displaystyle\mathcal{Y}=\Big\{(u,p,\eta,\theta)|\mathscr{D}(u,p,\eta,\theta)<\infty\Big\},\quad\|(u,p,\eta,\theta)\|_{\mathcal{Y}}=\left[\mathscr{D}(u,p,\eta,\theta)\right]^{1/2}.

By some minor modifications to
[
12
]
, we can directly establish the solutions to (
1.20
), which is stated in the following theorem.

Theorem 4.1
.

Assume that the forcing terms
F^{i,j}
satisfy the conditions in Appendix
B
and that
\mathfrak{K}(\eta)\leq\delta
is smaller than
\delta_{0}
, which is the same as in Theorem 4.7 in
[
12
]
. Suppose that the initial data
u_{0}
,
p_{0}
,
\theta_{0}
and
\eta_{0}
satisfy
\mathcal{E}(0)<\infty
and compatibility conditions in Appendix
B
.
Then for each
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
there exist
T_{\varepsilon}>0
such that for
0<T\leq T_{\varepsilon}
, then there exists a unique strong solution
(u,p,\xi,\theta)
to (
1.20
) on
[0,T]
such that

(u,p,\xi)\in\mathcal{X}\cap\mathcal{Y}
.
The triple
(D_{t}^{j}u,\partial_{t}^{j}p,\partial_{t}^{j}\xi,\partial_{t}^{j}\theta)
satisfies (
1.20
)
in the strong sense with initial data
(D_{t}^{j}u(0),\partial_{t}^{j}p(0),\partial_{t}^{j}\xi(0),\partial_{t}^{j}\theta(0))
for
j=0,1
and
(D_{t}^{2}u,\partial_{t}^{2}\xi,\partial_{t}^{2}\theta)
solves (
1.20
) in the pressureless weak sense for
j=2
. Moreover,
(u,p,\xi,\theta)
satisfy the estimate

(4.2)

\displaystyle\mathscr{K}(u,p,\xi,\theta)

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\left[1+\frac{T}{\varepsilon}\right]\exp\{\mathfrak{E}(\eta)T\}\bigg(\mathcal{E}(0)+\|\partial_{t}(F^{1}-F^{4}-F^{5})(0)\|_{(H^{1})^{\ast}}+\|F^{3}(0)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{t}(F^{8}+F^{9})(0)\|_{(H^{1})^{\ast}}^{2}+\|F^{1}(0)\|_{L^{q_{+}}}^{2}+\|F^{4}(0)\|_{W^{1-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}+\|F^{5}(0)\|_{W^{1-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|F^{8}(0)\|_{L^{q_{+}}}^{2}+\|F^{9}(0)\|_{W^{1-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}+\frac{T}{\varepsilon^{2}}\mathfrak{E}(\eta_{0})(\mathcal{E}(0)+\|\partial_{1}F^{3}(0)\|_{W^{1-1/q_{-},q_{-}}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|F^{4}(0)\|_{W^{1-1/q_{-},q_{-}}}^{2})+(1+\mathfrak{E}(\eta))(\|F^{1}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{q_{+}}}^{2}+\|F^{4}\|_{L^{\infty}W^{1-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|F^{5}\|_{L^{\infty}W^{1-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{1}F^{3}\|_{L^{\infty}W^{1-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}F^{1}\|_{L^{2}L^{q_{-}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}F^{4}\|_{L^{2}W^{1-1/q_{-},q_{-}}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{t}F^{5}\|_{L^{2}W^{1-1/q_{-},q_{-}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{1}\partial_{t}F^{3}\|_{L^{2}W^{1-1/q_{-},q_{-}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{2}(F^{1}-F^{4}-F^{5})\|_{(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{T})^{\ast}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|F^{8}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{q_{+}}}^{2}+\|F^{9}\|_{L^{\infty}W^{1-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}F^{8}\|_{L^{2}L^{q_{-}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}F^{9}\|_{L^{2}W^{1-1/q_{-},q_{-}}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{t}^{2}(F^{8}+F^{9})\|_{(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{T})^{\ast}}^{2})+\sum_{j=0}^{2}\|[F^{7,j}]_{\ell}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{T}{\varepsilon}\sum_{j=0}^{2}\|F^{3,j}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{0}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=0}^{2}\|F^{3,j}\|_{L^{2}H^{1/2-\alpha}}^{2}+\sum_{j=0}^{1}(\|\partial_{t}^{j}F^{1}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{0}}^{2}+\|[F^{7,j}]_{\ell}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}}^{2})\bigg),

where
C_{0}=C_{0}(g,\sigma,\ell,\Omega)
is a positive constant independent of
\varepsilon
.

4.2.
Preliminaries for the nonlinear system

We now give some preparations for the local well-posedness of the nonlinear
\varepsilon-
regularized system (
1.21
).

From the formulation of (
1.11
), we have the nonlinear interaction terms as the forcing given by Appendix
A
.
In order to close the energy estimates, we need to control the forcing terms in the sense of Theorem
3.9
and
3.12
. We first estimate the forcing terms in the bulk.

Proposition 4.2
.

It holds that

(4.3)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \|F^{8}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}+\|\partial_{t}F^{8}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}+\|\partial_{t}(F^{8}+F^{9})\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}^{1})^{\ast}}\lesssim\mathfrak{E}+\mathfrak{E}^{3/2},\\
\|\partial_{t}F^{8}\|_{L^{2}L^{q_{-}}}+\|\partial_{t}^{2}(F^{8}+F^{9})\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{H}^{1})^{\ast}}\lesssim\mathfrak{E}^{1/2}\mathfrak{D}^{1/2}.\end{aligned}

Proof.

It is directly to combine the Hölder inequality, Sobolev embedding estimates and trace theory, and the bounds of
K
and
\mathcal{A}
to estimate

(4.4)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \|F^{8}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}&\lesssim(\|\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t,x}}+\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{t,x}})\|\nabla\theta\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim(\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{L^{\infty}H^{3/2+(\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha)/2}}+\|u\|_{L^{\infty}W^{2,q_{+}}})\|\theta\|_{L^{\infty}W^{2,q_{+}}}.\end{aligned}

Then we consider
\partial_{t}F^{1}=\partial_{t}^{2}\bar{\eta}W\psi_{2}\theta+\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}W\partial_{t}K\psi_{2}\theta+\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}W\psi_{2}\partial_{t}\theta-\partial_{t}u\cdot\nabla_{\mathcal{A}}\theta-u\cdot\nabla_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}}\theta-u\cdot\nabla_{\mathcal{A}}\partial_{t}\theta
.
The similar argument as
F^{8}
enables us to obtain

(4.5)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \|\partial_{t}F^{8}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{0}}&\lesssim(\|\partial_{t}^{2}\bar{\eta}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,t}}+\|\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,t}}\|\nabla\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,t}}+\|\nabla\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,t}}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,t}}+\|\partial_{t}u\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,t}})\|\nabla\theta\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}\\
&\quad+(\|\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,t}}+\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,t}})\|\nabla\partial_{t}\theta\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim(\|\partial_{t}^{2}\eta\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}+\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{L^{\infty}H^{3/2+(\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha)/2}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}u\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1+\varepsilon_{-}/2}})\|\theta\|_{L^{\infty}W^{2,q_{+}}}\\
&\quad+\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{L^{\infty}H^{3/2+(\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha)/2}}(\|\theta\|_{L^{\infty}W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\theta\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1+\varepsilon_{-}/2}}).\end{aligned}

Then it is trivial that
\|\partial_{t}(F^{8}+F^{9})\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}^{1})^{\ast}}\leq\|\partial_{t}F^{8}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}
,
since
F^{9}=0
.

In addition, we bound the
\|\partial_{t}F^{8}\|_{L^{2}L^{q_{-}}}
by

(4.6)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}F^{8}\|_{L^{2}L^{q_{-}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial_{t}^{2}\bar{\eta}\|_{L^{2}L^{2/\alpha}}\|\nabla\theta\|_{L^{\infty}L^{1/(1-(\varepsilon_{-}+\alpha)/2)}}+\|\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,t}}(\|\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}\|\theta\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}+\|\partial_{t}\theta\|_{L^{2}H^{1}})

\displaystyle+(\|\partial_{t}u\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,t}}+\|\nabla\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,t}}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,t}})\|\theta\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}+\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,t}}\|\partial_{t}\theta\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\|\partial_{t}\theta\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}+\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{L^{\infty}H^{3/2+(\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha)/2}}\|\theta\|_{L^{2}H^{1}})\|u\|_{L^{\infty}W^{2,q_{+}}}

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{L^{\infty}H^{3/2+(\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha)/2}}(\|\theta\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}+\|\partial_{t}\theta\|_{L^{2}H^{1}})+\|\partial_{t}u\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1+\varepsilon_{-}/2}}\|\theta\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{t}^{2}\eta\|_{L^{2}H^{3/2-\alpha}}\|\theta\|_{L^{\infty}W^{2,q_{+}}}.

For
\partial_{t}^{2}F^{8}
, we consider

(4.7)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \left<\partial_{t}^{2}F^{8},\psi\right>_{\ast}&=\int_{\Omega}(\partial_{t}^{3}\bar{\eta}WK\psi_{2}\theta+2\partial_{t}^{2}\bar{\eta}W\partial_{t}K\psi_{2}\theta+2\partial_{t}^{2}\bar{\eta}WK\psi_{2}\partial_{t}\theta)\cdot\psi\\
&\quad+\int_{\Omega}(\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}\partial_{t}^{2}KW\psi_{2}\theta+2\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}W\partial_{t}K\psi_{2}\partial_{t}\theta+\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}WK\psi_{2}\partial_{t}^{2}\theta)\cdot\psi\\
&\quad-\int_{\Omega}(\partial_{t}^{2}u\cdot\nabla_{\mathcal{A}}\theta+2\partial_{t}u\cdot\nabla_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}}\theta+2\partial_{t}u\cdot\nabla_{\mathcal{A}}\partial_{t}\theta)\cdot\psi J\\
&\quad-\int_{\Omega}(u\cdot\nabla_{\partial_{t}^{2}\mathcal{A}}\theta+2u\cdot\nabla_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}}\partial_{t}\theta+u\cdot\nabla_{\mathcal{A}}\partial_{t}^{2}\theta)\cdot\psi J\end{aligned}

for any
\psi\in H^{1}(\Omega)
. We first use Hölder inequality and the finite volume of
\Omega
to see that

(4.8)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \left<\partial_{t}^{2}F^{8},\psi\right>_{(\mathcal{H}^{1})^{\ast}}&\lesssim\|\nabla\theta\|_{L^{2/(1-\varepsilon_{+})}}\Big(\|\partial_{t}^{3}\bar{\eta}\|_{L^{2/\varepsilon_{+}}}+\|\partial_{t}^{2}\bar{\eta}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}\|_{W^{1,\infty}}+\|\partial_{t}^{2}\bar{\eta}\|_{W^{1,2/\alpha}}\|\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}\|_{L^{\infty}}\\
&\quad+\|\partial_{t}^{2}u\|_{L^{2/\varepsilon_{+}}}+\|\partial_{t}u\|_{L^{2/\varepsilon_{+}}}\|\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}\|_{W^{1,\infty}}+\|\partial_{t}^{2}\bar{\eta}\|_{W^{1,2/\alpha}}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}}\Big)\|\psi\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\quad+\|\nabla\partial_{t}\theta\|_{H^{0}}(\|\partial_{t}^{2}\bar{\eta}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}\|_{W^{1,\infty}}\|\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\partial_{t}u\|_{L^{\infty}}\\
&\quad+\|u\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}\|_{W^{1,\infty}})\|\psi\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla\partial_{t}^{2}\theta\|_{H^{0}}(\|u\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}\|_{L^{\infty}})\|\psi\|_{L^{2}}.\end{aligned}

Then we employ Sobolev embedding estimates and trace theory with
\mathfrak{E}
small to deduce

(4.9)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \left<\partial_{t}^{2}F^{8},\psi\right>_{\ast}&\lesssim\|\theta\|_{L^{\infty}W^{2,q_{+}}}(\|\partial_{t}^{3}\eta\|_{L^{2}H^{1/2}}+\|\partial_{t}^{2}\eta\|_{L^{2}H^{3/2-\alpha}}+\|\partial_{t}u\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}+\|\partial_{t}^{2}u\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}\\
&\quad+\|u\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{-}}})\|\psi\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}+\|\partial_{t}\theta\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}(\|\partial_{t}^{2}\eta\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}+\|\partial_{t}u\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1+\varepsilon_{-}/2}}\\
&\quad+\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{L^{\infty}H^{3/2+(\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha)/2}}(\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{L^{\infty}H^{3/2+(\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha)/2}}+\|u\|_{L^{\infty}W^{2,q_{+}}}))\|\psi\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}\\
&\quad+\|\partial_{t}^{2}\theta\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}(\|u\|_{L^{\infty}W^{2,q_{+}}}+\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{L^{\infty}H^{3/2+(\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha)/2}})\|\psi\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}.\end{aligned}

Hence the completion of proof by combining all the above inequalities and the definition of
\mathfrak{E}
and
\mathfrak{D}
.
∎

4.3.
Initial Data for the Nonlinear
\varepsilon
-Regularized System

We assume that the initial data

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} (u_{0},p_{0},\theta_{0},\eta_{0},\partial_{t}u(0),\partial_{t}p(0),\partial_{t}\theta(0),\partial_{t}\eta(0),\partial_{t}^{2}u(0),\partial_{t}^{2}\theta(0),\partial_{t}^{2}\eta(0),\varepsilon\partial_{t}\eta(0),\sqrt{\varepsilon}\partial_{t}^{2}\eta(0),\varepsilon\partial_{t}^{2}\eta(0))\end{aligned}

for (
1.11
) are in the space
X^{\varepsilon}
defined via

(4.10)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} X^{\varepsilon}:&=W^{2,q_{+}}(\Omega)\times W^{1,q_{+}}(\Omega)\times W^{2,q_{+}}(\Omega)\times W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}(\Sigma)\times H^{1+\varepsilon_{-}/2}(\Omega)\times H^{0}(\Omega)\\
&\times H^{1+\varepsilon_{-}/2}(\Omega)\times H^{3/2+(\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha)/2}(\Sigma)\times H^{0}(\Omega)\times H^{0}(\Sigma)\times H^{1}(\Sigma)\times W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}(\Sigma)\\
&\times H^{3/2-\alpha}(\Sigma)\times W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}(\Sigma)\end{aligned}

and satisfy the compatibility conditions (
1.16
)
as well as zero average conditions (
1.17
),
where
X^{\varepsilon}
is a Banach space, with the square norm

(4.11)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\|(u_{0},p_{0},\theta_{0},\eta_{0},\partial_{t}u(0),\partial_{t}p(0),\partial_{t}\theta(0),\partial_{t}\eta(0),\partial_{t}^{2}u(0),\partial_{t}^{2}\theta(0),\partial_{t}^{2}\eta(0),\partial_{t}^{3}\eta(0))\|_{X^{\varepsilon}}^{2}\\
&=\mathcal{E}(0)+\varepsilon^{2}\|\partial_{t}\eta(0)\|_{W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\|\partial_{t}^{2}\eta(0)\|_{W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}+\varepsilon\|\partial_{t}^{2}\eta(0)\|_{H^{3/2-\alpha}}^{2},\end{aligned}

where
\mathcal{E}(0)
is defined via (
1.14
),

From the Theorem
B.1
, we have known that our initial data really exist, provided that
\|\partial_{t}^{2}u(0)\|_{H^{0}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{2}\eta(0)\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\|\partial_{t}\eta(0)\|_{W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{2}\eta(0)\|_{W^{2-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\|\partial_{t}^{2}\eta(0)\|_{W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}+\varepsilon\|\partial_{t}^{2}\eta(0)\|_{H^{3/2-\alpha}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{2}\theta(0)\|_{H^{0}}^{2}
is sufficiently small.

4.4.
Existence of Solutions to nonlinear
\varepsilon
–regularized system

We now consider the local well-posedness of the nonlinear problem prescribed by (
1.21
). Our strategy is to
work in a complete metric space, that requires high regularity bounds,
but that is endowed with a metric only involves low-regularity. First we will find a complete metric space, endowed with a weak choice of a metric, compatible with the linear estimates in Theorem
3.12
. Then we will prove that the fixed point on this metric space gives a solution to (
1.21
).

Definition 4.1
.

Suppose that
T>0
. For
\delta\in(0,\infty)
we define the space

(4.12)

\displaystyle S(T,\delta)

\displaystyle=\Big\{(u,p,\theta,\eta):\Omega\to\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\Big|(u,p,\theta,\eta))\in\mathcal{X}\cap\mathcal{Y},\ \text{with}\ \mathfrak{K}(u,p,\theta,\eta)^{1/2}\leq\delta

\displaystyle\text{and}\ (u,p,\theta,\eta)\ \text{achieve the initial data in Appendix \ref{sec:initial}}\Big\}.

We endow this space with the metric

(4.13)

\displaystyle d((u,p,\theta,\eta),(v,q,\vartheta,\xi))

\displaystyle=\|u-v\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}+\|u-v\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}+\|u-v\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{+}}}+\|p-q\|_{L^{2}W^{1,q_{+}}}

\displaystyle+\|\theta-\vartheta\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}+\|\theta-\vartheta\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}+\|\theta-\vartheta\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{+}}}+\|\eta-\xi\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}

\displaystyle+\|\eta-\xi\|_{L^{2}H^{3/2-\alpha}}+\|\eta-\xi\|_{L^{2}W^{3-1/q_{+}.q_{+}}}+\|\partial_{t}u-\partial_{t}v\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{t}u-\partial_{t}v\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}+\|\partial_{t}\eta-\partial_{t}\xi\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}+\|\partial_{t}\eta-\partial_{t}\xi\|_{L^{2}H^{3/2-\alpha}}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon\|\partial_{t}^{2}\eta-\partial_{t}^{2}\xi\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}+\varepsilon^{1/2}\|\partial_{t}\eta-\partial_{t}\xi\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}

\displaystyle+\|[\partial_{t}\eta-\partial_{t}\xi]_{\ell}\|_{L^{2}([0,T])}+\|[\partial_{t}^{2}\eta-\partial_{t}^{2}\xi]_{\ell}\|_{L^{2}([0,T])}

for any
(u,p,\eta),(v,q,\xi)\in S(T,\delta)
, where the temporal norm is evaluated on the set
[0,T]
.

It is easy to see that the space
S(T,\delta)
is complete for each fixed
\delta
. Now we employ the metric space
S(T,\delta)
and a contraction mapping argument to construct a sequence of approximate solutions
(u^{\varepsilon},p^{\varepsilon},\theta^{\varepsilon},\eta^{\varepsilon})
for each
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
satisfying (
1.21
). For simplicity, the superscript
\varepsilon
will be typically suppressed in the notations and we denote the unknown as
(u,p,\theta,\eta)
instead of
(u^{\varepsilon},p^{\varepsilon},\theta^{\varepsilon},\eta^{\varepsilon})
.

Theorem 4.3
.

Suppose that there exists a universal constant
\delta>0
sufficiently small such that the initial data for the nonlinear system are given as in Section
4.3
satisfying

(4.14)

\displaystyle\|(u_{0},p_{0},\theta_{0},\eta_{0},\partial_{t}u(0),\partial_{t}p(0),\partial_{t}\theta(0),\partial_{t}\eta(0),\partial_{t}^{2}u(0),\partial_{t}^{2}\theta(0),\partial_{t}^{2}\eta(0),\partial_{t}^{3}\eta(0))\|_{X^{\varepsilon}}^{2}\leq\delta.

Then there exists a constant
C>0
such that for each
0<\varepsilon\leq\min\{1,1/(8C)\}
there exists a unique solution
(u,p,\theta,\eta)
to (
1.21
), belonging to the metric space
S(T_{\varepsilon},\delta)
, where
T_{\varepsilon}>0
is sufficiently small. In particular
(u,p,\theta,\eta)\in\mathcal{X}\cap\mathcal{Y}
, where
\mathcal{X}
and
\mathcal{Y}
are defined in (
4.1
).

Proof.

Step 1 – Solving the Linear Problem

Suppose that
\mathfrak{K}(u,p,\theta,\eta)\leq\delta^{2}
is sufficiently small. For every
(u,p,\theta,\eta)\in S(T_{\varepsilon},\delta)
given, let
(\tilde{u},\tilde{p},\tilde{\theta},\tilde{\eta})
instead of
(u,p,\theta,\xi)
be the unique solution to the linear problems (
1.21
).
Then we apply the Theorem
3.9
and
3.12
to system (
1.21
), with the Propositions
4.2
under the assumption on
\mathfrak{K}(u,p,\theta,\eta)
to obtain that

(4.15)

\mathfrak{K}(\tilde{u},\tilde{p},\tilde{\theta},\tilde{\eta})\leq C_{0}\Big[1+\frac{T}{\varepsilon}\Big]e^{T\delta}\left(C_{1}\Big(1+\frac{T}{\varepsilon^{2}}\Big)\mathcal{E}(0)+C_{2}\Big(1+\frac{T}{\varepsilon}\Big)\delta^{4}\right),

for some universal constants
C_{0}
,
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
. We first choose
T=T_{\varepsilon}
such that
T_{\varepsilon}\leq\varepsilon^{2}
for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
. Then we choose
\delta\in(0,1)
such that
4C_{0}C_{2}e\delta^{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}
. Finally, we restrict the initial data
\mathcal{E}(0)
so that
4C_{0}C_{1}e\mathcal{E}(0)\leq\frac{1}{2}\delta^{2}
. Consequently, we have
\mathfrak{K}(\tilde{u},\tilde{p},\tilde{\theta},\tilde{\eta})\leq\delta^{2}
so that
(\tilde{u},\tilde{p},\tilde{\theta},\tilde{\eta})\in S(T_{\varepsilon},\delta)
.

Step 2 – PDEs for the Differences

Define the operator

(4.16)

A:S(T_{\varepsilon},\delta)\rightarrow S(T_{\varepsilon},\delta)

via
A(u,p,\theta,\eta)=(\tilde{u},\tilde{p},\tilde{\theta},\tilde{\eta})
for each
(u,p,\theta,\eta)\in S(T_{\varepsilon},\delta)
.

In order to show that the operator
A
is contract on
S(T_{\varepsilon},\delta)
, we choose two elements
(u^{i},p^{i},\theta^{i},\eta^{i})\in S(T_{\varepsilon},\delta)
, and define
A(u^{i},p^{i},\theta^{i},\eta^{i})=(\tilde{u}^{i},\tilde{p}^{i},\tilde{\theta}^{i},\tilde{\eta}^{i})
as above,
i=1,2
. For simplicity, we will abuse notation and denote
u=u^{1}-u^{2}
,
p=p^{1}-p^{2}
,
\theta=\theta^{1}-\theta^{2}
,
\eta=\eta^{1}-\eta^{2}
and the same for
\tilde{u},\tilde{p},\tilde{\theta},\tilde{\eta}
. From the difference of equation for
(\tilde{u}^{i},\tilde{p}^{i},\tilde{\theta}^{i},\tilde{\eta}^{i})
,
i=1,2
, we know that

(4.17)

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}+k\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\mathcal{A}^{1}}\nabla_{\mathcal{A}^{1}}\tilde{\theta}=k\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\mathcal{A}^{1}}(\nabla_{\mathcal{A}^{1}-\mathcal{A}^{2}}\tilde{\theta}^{2})+R^{8}\quad&\text{in}&\quad\Omega,\\
&k\nabla_{\mathcal{A}^{1}}\tilde{\theta}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{1}+\tilde{\theta}|\mathcal{N}^{1}|=k\nabla_{\mathcal{A}^{1}-\mathcal{A}^{2}}\tilde{\theta}^{2}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{1}+R^{9}\quad&\text{on}&\quad\Sigma,\\
&\tilde{\theta}=0\quad&\text{on}&\quad\Sigma_{s}\end{aligned}\right.

with zero initial data, where
R^{8}
and
R^{9}
are defined by

(4.18)

\displaystyle R^{8}

\displaystyle=k\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{(\mathcal{A}^{1}-\mathcal{A}^{2})}(\nabla_{\mathcal{A}^{2}}\tilde{\theta}^{2})-(\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}^{1}-\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}^{2})K^{1}W\partial_{2}\theta^{1}+\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}^{2}(K^{1}-K^{2})W\partial_{2}\theta^{1}

\displaystyle+\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}^{2}K^{2}W\partial_{2}\theta+u\cdot\nabla_{\mathcal{A}^{1}}\theta^{1}+u^{2}\cdot\nabla_{\mathcal{A}^{1}-\mathcal{A}^{2}}\theta^{1}+u^{2}\cdot\nabla_{\mathcal{A}^{2}}\theta,

\displaystyle R^{9}

\displaystyle=k\nabla_{\mathcal{A}^{2}}\tilde{\theta}^{2}\cdot(\mathcal{N}^{1}-\mathcal{N}^{2})+\tilde{\theta}^{2}|\mathcal{N}^{1}-\mathcal{N}^{2}|

and
\mathcal{A}^{i}
,
\mathcal{N}^{i}
,
J^{i}
and
K^{i}
are in terms of
\eta^{i}
,
i=1,2
.

We now focus on (
4.17
). The solutions are regular enough to be differentiated in time to result in the system

(4.19)

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{t}^{2}\tilde{\theta}-k\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\mathcal{A}^{1}}\nabla_{\mathcal{A}^{1}}\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}=k\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\mathcal{A}^{1}}(\nabla_{\mathcal{A}^{1}-\mathcal{A}^{2}}\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}^{2})+k\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\mathcal{A}^{1}}(\nabla_{\partial_{t}(\mathcal{A}^{1}-\mathcal{A}^{2})}\tilde{\theta}^{2})\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+k\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\mathcal{A}^{1}}\nabla_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}^{1}}\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}+R^{8,1}\quad&\text{in}&\quad\Omega,\\
&k\nabla_{\mathcal{A}^{1}}\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{1}+\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}|\mathcal{N}^{1}|=-k\nabla_{\mathcal{A}^{1}-\mathcal{A}^{2}}\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}^{2}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{1}-k\nabla_{\partial_{t}(\mathcal{A}^{1}-\mathcal{A}^{2})}\tilde{\theta}^{2}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{1}\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad-k\nabla_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}^{1}}\tilde{\theta}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{1}+R^{9,1}\quad&\text{on}&\quad\Sigma,\\
&\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}=0\quad&\text{on}&\quad\Sigma_{s}\end{aligned}\right.

with zero initial data, where
R^{8,1}
,
R^{9,1}
are defined by

(4.20)

\displaystyle R^{8,1}

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}R^{8}+k\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}^{1}}(\nabla_{(\mathcal{A}^{1}-\mathcal{A}^{2})}\tilde{\theta}^{2})+k\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}^{1}}(\nabla_{\mathcal{A}^{1}}\tilde{\theta}),

\displaystyle R^{9,1}

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}R^{9}-k\nabla_{\mathcal{A}^{1}}\tilde{\theta}\cdot\partial_{t}\mathcal{N}^{1}-\tilde{\theta}\partial_{t}|\mathcal{N}^{1}|-k\nabla_{\mathcal{A}^{1}-\mathcal{A}^{2}}\tilde{\theta}^{2}\cdot\partial_{t}\mathcal{N}^{1}.

Step 3 – Energy Estimates for
\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}

By multiplying the first equation of (
4.19
) by
\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}J^{1}
, integrating over
\Omega
and integrating by parts, we have the equation

(4.21)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}\frac{|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}|^{2}}{2}J^{1}+\frac{k}{2}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla_{\mathcal{A}^{1}}\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}|^{2}J^{1}+\int_{\Sigma}|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}|^{2}|\mathcal{N}^{1}|

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\frac{|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}|^{2}}{2}\partial_{t}J^{1}-\frac{k}{2}\int_{\Omega}\left(\nabla_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}^{1}-\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}^{2}}\tilde{\theta}^{2}+\nabla_{\mathcal{A}^{1}-\mathcal{A}^{2}}\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}^{2}+\nabla_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}^{1}}\tilde{\theta}\right)\cdot\nabla_{\mathcal{A}^{1}}\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}J^{1}

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega}R^{8,1}\cdot\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}J^{1}+\int_{-\ell}^{\ell}R^{9,1}\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}.

We now estimate the terms in right-hand side of (
4.21
) and below. For terms in bulk, by definition in (
4.18
), we first handle terms involving
\partial_{t}R^{8}
:

(4.22)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}R^{8}\cdot\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}J^{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\Omega}|\nabla\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}|(|\nabla^{2}\bar{\eta}^{2}||\nabla\tilde{\theta}^{2}|+|\nabla^{2}\tilde{\theta}^{2}|)|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}|+|\nabla\bar{\eta}|(|\nabla^{2}\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}^{2}||\nabla\tilde{\theta}^{2}|+|\nabla\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}^{2}||\nabla^{2}\tilde{\theta}^{2}|)|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}|

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega}|\nabla\bar{\eta}|(|\nabla^{2}\bar{\eta}^{2}||\nabla\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}^{2}|+|\nabla^{2}\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}^{2}|)|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}|+((|\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}^{1}|+|\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}^{2}|)|\nabla\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}|+|\partial_{t}^{2}\bar{\eta}^{2}||\nabla\bar{\eta}|)|\partial_{2}\theta^{1}||\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}|

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega}(|\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}|+|\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}^{2}||\nabla\bar{\eta}|)|\partial_{2}\partial_{t}\theta^{1}||\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}|+(|\partial_{t}^{2}\bar{\eta}^{2}|+|\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}^{2}||\nabla\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}^{2}|)|\partial_{2}\theta||\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}|+|\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}^{2}||\partial_{2}\partial_{t}\theta||\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}|

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega}(|\partial_{t}u|+|u||\nabla\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}^{1}|+|\partial_{t}u^{2}||\nabla\bar{\eta}|+|u^{2}||\nabla\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}|)|\nabla\theta^{1}||\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}|+(|u|+|u^{2}||\nabla\bar{\eta}|)|\nabla\partial_{t}\theta^{1}||\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}|

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega}(|\partial_{t}u^{2}|+|u^{2}||\nabla\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}^{2}|)|\nabla\theta||\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}|+|u^{2}||\nabla\partial_{t}\theta||\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}|+|\partial_{t}^{2}\bar{\eta}||\partial_{2}\theta^{1}||\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}|,

where we have used
\|\mathcal{A}^{i}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|J^{i}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|K^{i}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 1
,
i=1,2
.
Then by Hölder inequality, Sobolev embedding and trace theory, after integrating over
[0,T]
, the second line in (
4.31
) is bounded by

(4.23)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}\|_{L^{2/\alpha}}(\|\nabla^{2}\bar{\eta}^{2}\|_{L^{2/(1-\varepsilon_{+})}}\|\nabla\tilde{\theta}^{2}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla^{2}\tilde{\theta}^{2}\|_{L^{q_{+}}})\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2/(\varepsilon_{+}-\alpha)}}

\displaystyle+\|\nabla\bar{\eta}\|_{L^{4/(\alpha-\varepsilon_{-})}}\|\nabla^{2}\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}^{2}\|_{L^{4/(2-\varepsilon_{-}+\alpha)}}\|\nabla\tilde{\theta}^{2}\|_{L^{2/(1-\varepsilon_{+})}}\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2/\varepsilon_{+}}}

\displaystyle+\|\nabla\bar{\eta}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}^{2}\|_{L^{2/(\varepsilon_{+}-\varepsilon_{-})}}\|\nabla^{2}\tilde{\theta}^{2}\|_{L^{q_{+}}}\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2/\varepsilon_{-}}},

so that we have the estimate

(4.24)

\displaystyle\eqref{est:remain_bulk12}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{3/2-\alpha}(\|\eta^{2}\|_{W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}\|\tilde{\theta}^{2}\|_{1}+\|\tilde{\theta}^{2}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}})\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{1}

\displaystyle+\|\eta\|_{W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}\|\partial_{t}\eta^{2}\|_{3/2+(\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha)/2}\|\tilde{\theta}^{2}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta^{1/2}(\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{L^{2}H^{3/2-\alpha}}+\|\eta\|_{L^{2}W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}})\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}.

The third line in (
4.31
), after integrating over
[0,T]
in time, are estimated by

(4.25)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\bar{\eta}\|_{L^{2/\alpha}}\bigg(\|\nabla^{2}\bar{\eta}^{2}\|_{L^{2/(1-\varepsilon_{+})}}\|\nabla\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}^{2}\|_{L^{4/(2-\varepsilon_{-})}}\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{4/(2\varepsilon_{+}+\varepsilon_{-}-2\alpha)}}+\|\nabla^{2}\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}^{2}\|_{L^{q_{-}}}

\displaystyle\times\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2/(\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha)}}\bigg)+(\|\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}^{1}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}})\|\nabla\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}\|_{L^{2/\alpha}}\|\partial_{2}\theta^{1}\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2/(1-\alpha)}}

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{t}^{2}\bar{\eta}^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\bar{\eta}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial_{2}\theta^{1}\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\|\eta\|_{H^{3/2-\alpha}}(\|\bar{\eta}^{2}\|_{W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}^{2}\|_{H^{1+\varepsilon_{-}/2}}+\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}^{2}\|_{W^{2,q_{-}}})\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle+(\|\partial_{t}\eta^{1}\|_{3/2+(\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha)/2}+\|\partial_{t}\eta^{2}\|_{3/2+(\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha)/2})\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{3/2-\alpha})\|\theta^{1}\|_{1}\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{t}^{2}\eta^{2}\|_{1}\|\eta\|_{W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}\|\theta^{1}\|_{1}\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta^{1/2}(\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{L^{2}H^{3/2-\alpha}}+\|\eta\|_{L^{\infty}H^{3/2-\alpha}})\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}+\delta^{1/2}\|\eta\|_{L^{2}W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{0}},

where we have used the Sobolev embedding
H^{\varepsilon_{-}/2}\hookrightarrow L^{4/(2-\varepsilon_{-})}
and
H^{1}\hookrightarrow L^{4/(2\varepsilon_{+}+\varepsilon_{-}-2\alpha)}
for bounded domain
\Omega
.
The fourth line in (
4.31
), after integrating over
[0,T]
in time (without loss of generality, we may assume
T<1
), are estimated by

(4.26)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}(\|\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\bar{\eta}\|_{L^{\infty}})\|\nabla\partial_{t}\theta^{1}\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{2}\theta\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}}(\|\partial_{t}^{2}\bar{\eta}^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}})+\|\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial_{2}\partial_{t}\theta\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}(\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{H^{1}}+\|\partial_{t}\eta^{1}\|_{H^{1}}\|\eta\|_{W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}})\|\partial_{t}\theta^{1}\|_{1+\varepsilon_{-}/2}\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\theta\|_{1}\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}}(\|\partial_{t}^{2}\eta^{2}\|_{1}

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{t}\eta^{2}\|_{1}\|\partial_{t}\eta^{2}\|_{3/2+(\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha)/2})+\|\partial_{t}\eta^{2}\|_{1}\|\partial_{t}\theta\|_{1}\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{T}\delta^{1/2}(\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}+\|\eta\|_{L^{2}W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}+\|\theta\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{+}}}+\|\partial_{t}\theta\|_{L^{2}H^{1}})\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{0}}.

After integrating over
[0,T]
in time, the fifth line in (
4.31
) are estimated by

(4.27)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}(\|\partial_{t}u\|_{L^{2}}+\|u\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}^{1}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\partial_{t}u^{2}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\bar{\eta}\|_{L^{\infty}})\|\nabla\theta^{1}\|_{L^{2/(1-\varepsilon_{+})}}\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2/\varepsilon_{+}}}

\displaystyle+\|u^{2}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}\|_{L^{2/\alpha}}\|\nabla\theta^{1}\|_{L^{2/(1-\varepsilon_{+})}}\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2/(\varepsilon_{+}-\alpha)}}+(\|u\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|u^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\bar{\eta}\|_{L^{\infty}})

\displaystyle\times\|\nabla\partial_{t}\theta^{1}\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}(\|\partial_{t}u\|_{L^{2}}+\|u\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{t}\eta^{1}\|_{3/2+(\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha)/2}+\|\partial_{t}u^{2}\|_{L^{2}}\|\eta\|_{W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}})\|\theta^{1}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{1}

\displaystyle+(\|u\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}+\|u^{2}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}\|\eta\|_{W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}})\|\partial_{t}\theta^{1}\|_{1}\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\|u^{2}\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{3/2-\alpha}\|\theta^{1}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{1},

so that

(4.28)

\displaystyle\eqref{est:remain_bulk15_1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta^{1/2}(\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{L^{2}H^{3/2-\alpha}}+\|\eta\|_{L^{2}W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}+\|u\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{+}}}+\|\partial_{t}u\|_{L^{2}H^{1}})\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}

\displaystyle+\delta^{1/2}(\|\eta\|_{L^{2}W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}+\|u\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{+}}})\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{0}},

Similarly, the sixth line in (
4.31
) are estimated by

(4.29)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}(\|\partial_{t}u^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|u^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}})\|\nabla\theta\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}}+\|u^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\partial_{t}\theta\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{t}^{2}\bar{\eta}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial_{2}\theta^{1}\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}},

so that

(4.30)

\displaystyle\eqref{est:remain_bulk16}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}(\|\partial_{t}u^{2}\|_{1+\varepsilon_{-}/2}+\|u^{2}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}\|\partial_{t}\eta^{2}\|_{3/2+(\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha)/2})\|\theta\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\|u^{2}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}\|\partial_{t}\theta\|_{1}\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{t}^{2}\eta\|_{1}\|\theta^{1}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta^{1/2}(\sqrt{T}\|\partial_{t}^{2}\eta\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}+\|u\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{+}}}+\|\partial_{t}\theta\|_{L^{2}H^{1}})\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{0}},

We combine (
4.22
)–(
4.29
) to obtain that

(4.31)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}R^{1}\cdot\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}J^{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta^{1/2}(\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{L^{2}H^{3/2-\alpha}}+\|\eta\|_{L^{2}W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}+\|\eta\|_{L^{\infty}H^{3/2-\alpha}}+\|u\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{+}}}+\|\partial_{t}u\|_{L^{2}H^{1}})

\displaystyle\times\|\partial_{t}\tilde{u}\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}+\delta^{1/2}(\sqrt{T}\|\partial_{t}^{2}\eta\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}+\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}+\|\eta\|_{L^{2}W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}

\displaystyle+\|u\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{+}}}+\|\partial_{t}u\|_{L^{2}H^{1}})\|\partial_{t}\tilde{u}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{0}}.

We now estimate other terms in the bulk such that

(4.32)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}^{1}}(\nabla_{(\mathcal{A}^{1}-\mathcal{A}^{2})}\tilde{\theta}^{2})\cdot\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}J^{1}\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}^{1}|\left(|\nabla^{2}\bar{\eta}||\nabla\tilde{u}^{2}|+|\nabla\bar{\eta}||\nabla^{2}\tilde{\theta}^{2}|\right)|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}^{1}\|_{L^{\infty}}(\|\nabla^{2}\bar{\eta}\|_{L^{2/(1-\varepsilon_{+})}}\|\nabla\tilde{\theta}^{2}\|_{L^{2/(1-\varepsilon_{+})}}\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{1/\varepsilon_{+}}}+\|\nabla\bar{\eta}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla^{2}\tilde{\theta}^{2}\|_{L^{q_{+}}}\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2/\varepsilon_{+}}})

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial_{t}\eta^{1}\|_{3/2+(\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha)/2}\|\eta\|_{W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}\|\tilde{\theta}^{2}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{1}\lesssim\delta\|\eta\|_{L^{2}W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}H^{1}},

and

(4.33)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}^{1}}(\nabla_{\mathcal{A}^{1}}\tilde{\theta})\cdot\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}J^{1}\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}^{1}|\left(|\nabla^{2}\bar{\eta}^{1}||\nabla\tilde{\theta}|+|\nabla^{2}\tilde{\theta}|\right)|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}^{1}\|_{L^{\infty}}\big[\|\nabla^{2}\bar{\eta}^{1}\|_{L^{2/(1-\varepsilon_{+})}}\|\nabla\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2/(1-\varepsilon_{+})}}\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{1/\varepsilon_{+}}}+(\|\nabla^{2}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{q_{+}}})\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2/\varepsilon_{+}}}\big]

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial_{t}\eta^{1}\|_{3/2+(\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha)/2}\big[(1+\|\eta^{1}\|_{W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}})\|\tilde{\theta}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}\big]\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta^{1/2}\|\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{+}}}\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}.

Consequently, the result in (
4.31
), (
4.32
) and (
4.33
) imply that

(4.34)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}R^{8,1}\cdot\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}J^{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta^{1/2}(\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{L^{2}H^{3/2-\alpha}}+\|\eta\|_{L^{2}W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}+\|\eta\|_{L^{\infty}H^{3/2-\alpha}}+\|u\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{+}}}

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{t}u\|_{L^{2}H^{1}})\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}+\delta^{1/2}(\sqrt{T}\|\partial_{t}^{2}\eta\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}+\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}+\|\eta\|_{L^{2}W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}

\displaystyle+\|u\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{+}}}+\|\partial_{t}u\|_{L^{2}H^{1}})\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{0}}+\delta^{1/2}\|\tilde{u}\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{+}}}\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}

The integrals in terms of symmetric gradient of
\partial_{t}\tilde{u}
are estimated by

(4.35)

\displaystyle|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\left(\nabla_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}^{1}-\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}^{2}}\tilde{\theta}^{2}+\nabla_{\mathcal{A}^{1}-\mathcal{A}^{2}}\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}^{2}+\nabla_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}^{1}}\tilde{\theta}\right)\cdot\nabla_{\mathcal{A}^{1}}\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}J^{1}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}(\|\nabla\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}\|_{L^{2/\alpha}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\tilde{\theta}^{2}\|_{L^{2/(1-\varepsilon_{+})}}+\|\nabla\tilde{\eta}\|_{L^{2/\alpha}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}^{2}\|_{L^{2/(1-\varepsilon_{+})}}

\displaystyle+\|\nabla\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}^{1}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}})\|\nabla\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta^{1/2}(\|\eta\|_{L^{2}H^{3/2-\alpha}}+\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{L^{2}H^{3/2-\alpha}}+\|\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}H^{1}})\|\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}.

We now consider the integrals on the boundary. We first estimate the terms of
\partial_{t}R^{9}
:

(4.36)

\displaystyle|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{-\ell}^{\ell}\partial_{t}R^{9}\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\int_{-\ell}^{\ell}\left[(|\nabla\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}^{2}||\nabla\tilde{\theta}^{2}|+|\nabla\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}^{2}|)|\partial_{1}\eta|+(|\nabla\tilde{\theta}^{2}|)|\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\eta|\right]|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}|

\displaystyle+(|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}^{2}||\partial_{1}\eta|+|\tilde{\theta}^{2}||\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\eta|)|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}|.

The first line in the right-hand side of (
4.36
), by Hölder and Sobolev inequalities, are estimated by

(4.37)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\bigg[(\|\nabla\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\tilde{\theta}^{2}\|_{L^{1/(1-\varepsilon_{-})}(\Sigma)}+\|\nabla\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}^{2}\|_{L^{1/(1-\varepsilon_{-})}(\Sigma)})\bigg]\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{1/(\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha)}(\Sigma)}

\displaystyle\times\|\partial_{1}\eta\|_{L^{1/\alpha}}+\|\nabla\tilde{\theta}^{2}\|_{L^{1/(1-\varepsilon_{+})}(\Sigma)}\|\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\eta\|_{L^{1/\alpha}}\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{1/(\varepsilon_{+}-\alpha)}(\Sigma)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\bigg[(|\partial_{t}\eta^{2}\|_{3/2+(\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha)/2}\|\tilde{\theta}^{2}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}+\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}^{2}\|_{W^{2,q_{-}}})\bigg]\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{1}\|\eta\|_{3/2-\alpha}

\displaystyle+\|\tilde{\theta}^{2}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{3/2-\alpha}\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta^{1/2}(\|\eta\|_{L^{\infty}H^{3/2-\alpha}}+\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{L^{2}H^{3/2-\alpha}})\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}.

The second line in the right-hand side of (
4.36
), by the similar method, are bounded by

(4.38)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}(\|\partial_{t}\theta^{2}\|_{L^{1/(1-\varepsilon_{-})}}\|\partial_{1}\eta\|_{L^{1/\alpha}}+\|\theta^{2}\|_{L^{1/(1-\varepsilon_{-})}}\|\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\eta\|_{L^{1/\alpha}})\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{1/(\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha)}(\Sigma)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta^{1/2}(\|\eta\|_{L^{\infty}H^{3/2-\alpha}}+\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{L^{2}H^{3/2-\alpha}})\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}.

The combination of (
4.36
) and (
4.38
) gives the bound

(4.39)

\displaystyle|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{-\ell}^{\ell}\partial_{t}R^{9}\cdot\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}|\lesssim\delta^{1/2}(\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}+\|\eta\|_{L^{\infty}H^{3/2-\alpha}}+\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{L^{2}H^{3/2-\alpha}})\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}.

Similarly, we estimate

(4.40)

\displaystyle|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{-\ell}^{\ell}\nabla_{\mathcal{A}^{1}-\mathcal{A}^{2}}\tilde{\theta}^{2}\partial_{t}\mathcal{N}^{1}\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\int_{-\ell}^{\ell}|\nabla\bar{\eta}||\nabla\tilde{\theta}^{2}||\partial_{t}\partial_{1}\eta^{1}||\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\bar{\eta}\|_{L^{1/\alpha}}\|\nabla\tilde{\theta}^{2}\|_{L^{1/(1-\varepsilon_{+})}(\Sigma)}\|\partial_{t}\partial_{1}\eta^{1}\|_{L^{1/\alpha}}\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{1/(\varepsilon_{+}-2\alpha)}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\|\tilde{\eta}\|_{W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}\|\tilde{\theta}^{2}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}\|\partial_{t}\eta^{1}\|_{3/2+}\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta^{1/2}\|\tilde{\eta}\|_{L^{2}W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}.

So the results in (
4.39
) and (
4.40
) imply

(4.41)

\displaystyle|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{-\ell}^{\ell}R^{9,1}\cdot\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\lesssim\delta^{1/2}(\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}+\|\eta\|_{L^{\infty}H^{3/2-\alpha}}+\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{L^{2}H^{3/2-\alpha}}

\displaystyle+\|\tilde{\eta}\|_{L^{2}W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}+\|\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{+}}})\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}

After integrating (
4.21
) over
[0,T]
, we use the Cauchy inequality and Lemma (
1.3
), Gronwall’s Lemma along with (
4.34
), (
4.35
) and(
4.41
) to deduce the bound

(4.42)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{0}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}H^{0}(\Sigma)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq e^{C_{1}T\delta^{1/2}}C_{2}\delta(\|\eta\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}^{2}+\|\eta\|_{L^{2}W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\eta\|_{L^{2}H^{3/2-\alpha}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{L^{2}H^{3/2-\alpha}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}^{2}+T\|\partial_{t}^{2}\eta\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}^{2}+\|u\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}u\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}^{2}+\|\tilde{u}\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|\tilde{u}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{0}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\tilde{u}\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}^{2}+\|\tilde{\eta}\|_{L^{2}W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}^{2}+\|\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}),

where the universal constants
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
are determined by the estimates (
4.34
), (
4.35
) and (
4.41
).

Step 4 – Energy Estimates for
\tilde{\theta}

By multiplying the first equation of (
4.17
) by
\tilde{\theta}J^{1}
, integrating over
\Omega
and integrating by parts, we have the equation

(4.43)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}\frac{|\tilde{\theta}|^{2}}{2}J^{1}+\int_{\Omega}|\nabla_{\mathcal{A}^{1}}\tilde{\theta}|^{2}J^{1}+\int_{\Sigma}|\tilde{\theta}|^{2}|\mathcal{N}^{1}|

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\frac{|\tilde{\theta}|^{2}}{2}\partial_{t}J^{1}-\frac{\mu}{2}\int_{\Omega}\nabla_{\mathcal{A}^{1}-\mathcal{A}^{2}}\tilde{\theta}^{2}\cdot\nabla_{\mathcal{A}^{1}}\tilde{\theta}J^{1}+\int_{\Omega}R^{8}\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}J^{1}+\int_{-\ell}^{\ell}R^{9}\tilde{\theta}.

Then we use the same arguments as in Step 3 to estimate the terms in the third line of (
4.43
):

(4.44)

\displaystyle|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\frac{|\tilde{\theta}|^{2}}{2}\partial_{t}J^{1}-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\nabla_{\mathcal{A}^{1}-\mathcal{A}^{2}}\tilde{\theta}^{2}\cdot\nabla_{\mathcal{A}^{1}}\tilde{\theta}J^{1}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}^{1}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla\bar{\eta}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\tilde{\theta}^{2}\|_{H^{1}}\|\tilde{\theta}\|_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta^{1/2}(T\|\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{0}}^{2}+\|\eta\|_{L^{2}W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}\|\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}),

and

(4.45)

\displaystyle|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}R^{8}\tilde{\theta}J^{1}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\bar{\eta}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}(\|\nabla^{2}\tilde{\theta}^{2}\|_{L^{q_{+}}}\|\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2/\varepsilon_{+}}}+\|\nabla\tilde{\theta}^{2}\|_{L^{2/(1-\varepsilon_{+})}}\|\nabla^{2}\bar{\eta}^{2}\|_{L^{2/(1-\varepsilon_{+})}}\|\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{1/\varepsilon_{+}}})

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}(\|\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}+\|\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}^{1}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\bar{\eta}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)})\|\partial_{2}\theta^{1}\|_{L^{2}}\|\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}}+\|u^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\theta\|_{L^{2}}\|\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}^{1}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{2}\theta\|_{L^{2}}\|\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}}+(\|u\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|u^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\bar{\eta}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)})\|\nabla\theta^{1}\|_{L^{2}}\|\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta^{1/2}(\|\eta\|_{L^{2}W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}+\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}+\|u\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{+}}}+\|\theta\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{+}}})\|\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{0}}

\displaystyle+\delta^{1/2}(\|\eta\|_{L^{2}W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}\|\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}).

The term with
R^{9}
are estimated by

(4.46)

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{-\ell}^{\ell}R^{9}\cdot\tilde{\theta}\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\bar{\eta}^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma)}\|\nabla\tilde{\theta}^{2}\|_{L^{1/(1-\varepsilon_{+})}(\Sigma)}\|\partial_{1}\eta\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{1/\varepsilon_{+}}(\Sigma)}

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{1}\eta^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\|\partial_{1}\eta\|_{L^{2}}\|\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}(\Sigma)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta^{1/2}(\|\eta\|_{L^{2}W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}+\|\eta\|_{L^{2}H^{1}})\|\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}H^{1}},

Then by combining (
4.43
)–(
4.46
), we have

(4.47)

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{0}}^{2}+\|\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}^{2}+\|\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}H^{0}(\Sigma)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq e^{C_{4}T\delta^{1/2}}C_{5}\delta(\|\eta\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}^{2}+\|\eta\|_{L^{2}W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\eta\|_{L^{2}H^{3/2-\alpha}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|u\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\theta\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}),

for some uniform constants
C_{4}
and
C_{5}
.

Step 5 – Elliptic Estimates for
\tilde{\theta}

The elliptic estimates for (
4.17
) enjoys the esitmate

(4.48)

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|-\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\mathcal{A}^{1}}(\nabla_{\mathcal{A}^{1}-\mathcal{A}^{2}}\tilde{\theta}^{2})+R^{8}-\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}L^{q_{+}}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{A}^{1}-\mathcal{A}^{2}}\tilde{\theta}^{2}\mathcal{N}^{1}+R^{9}\|_{L^{2}W^{1-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}.

We now estimate the terms under the first line in (
4.48
). By
\|\mathcal{A}^{1}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 1
,

(4.49)

\displaystyle\|\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\mathcal{A}^{1}}(\nabla_{\mathcal{A}^{1}-\mathcal{A}^{2}}\tilde{\theta}^{2})\|_{L^{2}L^{q_{+}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\||\nabla\bar{\eta}|\nabla^{2}\tilde{\theta}^{2}\|_{L^{2}L^{q_{+}}}^{2}+\||\nabla^{2}\bar{\eta}|\nabla\tilde{\theta}^{2}\|_{L^{2}L^{q_{+}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla\bar{\eta}\|_{L^{2}L^{\infty}}^{2}\|\nabla^{2}\tilde{\theta}^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\nabla^{2}\bar{\eta}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}^{2}\|\nabla\tilde{\theta}^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2/(1-\varepsilon)}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\eta\|_{L^{2}W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}\|\tilde{\theta}^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta\|\eta\|_{L^{2}W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}.

The definition of
R^{1}
, with
\|K^{i}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 1
and
\delta<1
, shows that

(4.50)

\displaystyle\|R^{8}\|_{L^{2}L^{q_{+}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla\bar{\eta}\|_{L^{2}L^{\infty}(\Omega)}^{2}\|\nabla^{2}\tilde{\theta}^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}\|_{L^{2}L^{2}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\tilde{\theta}^{1}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2/(1-\varepsilon_{+})}}

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}\|\nabla\bar{\eta}\|_{L^{2}L^{\infty}(\Omega)}^{2}\|\nabla\tilde{\theta}^{1}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2/(1-\varepsilon_{+})}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}\|\nabla\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}L^{2/(1-\varepsilon_{+})}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|u\|_{L^{2}L^{2}}\|\nabla\tilde{\theta}^{1}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2/(1-\varepsilon_{+})}}+\|u^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}^{2}\|\nabla\bar{\eta}\|_{L^{2}L^{\infty}}^{2}\|\nabla\tilde{\theta}^{1}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2/(1-\varepsilon_{+})}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|u^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}^{2}\|\nabla\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}L^{2/(1-\varepsilon_{+})}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\eta\|_{L^{2}W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}\|\tilde{\theta}^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}+(\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{L^{2}H^{3/2-\alpha}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\eta^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{3/2}}^{2}\|\eta\|_{L^{2}W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|u\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}^{2}+\|u^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}^{2}\|\eta\|_{L^{2}W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2})\|\tilde{u}^{1}\|_{L^{\infty}W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}

\displaystyle+(\|\partial_{t}\eta^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{3/2}}^{2}+\|u^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}^{2})\|\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta(\|\eta\|_{L^{2}W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}+\|u\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}^{2}+\|\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}),

It is trivial that

(4.51)

\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}L^{q_{+}}}^{2}\lesssim T\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}^{2},\quad q_{+}<2.

Note that
W^{1,q_{+}}(\Sigma)
is also an algebra. Then it can be used to bound

(4.52)

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\mathcal{A}^{1}-\mathcal{A}^{2}}\tilde{\theta}^{2}\mathcal{N}^{1}\|_{L^{2}W^{1-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla\tilde{\theta}^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}W^{1-1/q_{+},q_{+}}(\Sigma)}^{2}\|\nabla\bar{\eta}\|_{L^{2}W^{1,q_{+}}(\Sigma)}^{2}\lesssim\delta\|\eta\|_{L^{2}W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2},

and

(4.53)

\displaystyle\|R^{9}\|_{L^{2}W^{1-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg(\|\mathcal{A}^{1}\|_{L^{\infty}W^{1,q_{+}}(\Sigma)}\|\nabla\tilde{\theta}^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}W^{1-1/q_{+},q_{+}}(\Sigma)}^{2}+\|\tilde{\theta}^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}W^{1,q_{+}}(\Sigma)}\bigg)\|\partial_{1}\eta\|_{L^{2}W^{1,q_{+}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\tilde{\theta}^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}\|\tilde{\eta}\|_{L^{2}W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta\|\tilde{\eta}\|_{L^{2}W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}.

The combination of (
4.48
)–(
4.53
), after restricting
\delta
to be smaller if necessary, implies that

(4.54)

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}\lesssim\delta(\|\eta\|_{L^{2}W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}+\|u\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}^{2}+\|\tilde{\eta}\|_{L^{2}W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2})+\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}^{2}.

Step 6 – Fixed Point

We now choose
T<T_{\varepsilon}<\varepsilon
for each
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
. Then the differences for Navier-Stokes equations are handled like
[
12
]
, which along with the estimates (
4.42
) and (
4.47
) allow us to get the estimate

(4.55)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}\tilde{u}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{0}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\tilde{u}\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}^{2}+\varepsilon\|\partial_{t}^{2}\tilde{\eta}\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}^{2}+\|[\partial_{t}^{2}\tilde{\eta}]_{\ell}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}\|_{L^{2}H^{3/2-\alpha}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|\tilde{u}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}^{2}+\|\tilde{u}\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}^{2}+\|\tilde{\eta}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}^{2}+\|[\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}]_{\ell}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\tilde{\eta}\|_{L^{2}H^{3/2-\alpha}}^{2}+\|\tilde{p}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{0}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{0}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}^{2}+\|\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}^{2}+\|\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}^{2}+\|\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|\tilde{u}\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\tilde{p}\|_{L^{2}W^{1,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\tilde{\eta}\|_{L^{2}W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}\|_{L^{2}W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\delta^{1/2}(\|\eta\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}^{2}+\|\eta\|_{L^{2}W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\eta\|_{L^{2}H^{3/2-\alpha}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{L^{2}H^{3/2-\alpha}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}^{2}+\varepsilon\|\partial_{t}^{2}\eta\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|u\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}u\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}^{2}+\|u\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}^{2}+\|[\partial_{t}^{2}\eta]_{\ell}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|[\partial_{t}\eta]_{\ell}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\theta\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\theta\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|\theta\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}^{2}+\|\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{0}}^{2}+\|\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}^{2}+\|\tilde{u}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{0}}^{2}+\|\tilde{u}\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\tilde{u}\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|\tilde{p}\|_{L^{2}W^{1,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\tilde{p}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{0}}^{2}+\|\tilde{\eta}\|_{L^{2}W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\|\tilde{\eta}\|_{L^{2}W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}+\|[\partial_{t}^{2}\tilde{\eta}]_{\ell}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}),

where
C
is a universal constant allowed to change from line to line, and we have used
\delta<\frac{1}{2}
. Then we might restrict
\delta
to be smaller such that all the terms with superscript
\widetilde{}
below the sign of inequality in (
4.55
) be absorbed into terms above the sign of inequality in (
4.55
). Moreover, we might restrict
\delta
to be smaller again so that we can deduce the following inequality:

(4.56)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}\tilde{u}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{0}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\tilde{u}\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}^{2}+\varepsilon\|\partial_{t}^{2}\tilde{\eta}\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}^{2}+\|[\partial_{t}^{2}\tilde{\eta}]_{\ell}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}\|_{L^{2}H^{3/2-\alpha}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|\tilde{u}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}^{2}+\|\tilde{u}\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}^{2}+\|\tilde{\eta}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}^{2}+\|[\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}]_{\ell}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\tilde{\eta}\|_{L^{2}H^{3/2-\alpha}}^{2}+\|\tilde{p}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{0}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{0}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}^{2}+\|\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}^{2}+\|\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}^{2}+\|\tilde{\theta}\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|\tilde{u}\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\tilde{p}\|_{L^{2}W^{1,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\tilde{\eta}\|_{L^{2}W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}\|_{L^{2}W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{C}(\|\eta\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}^{2}+\|\eta\|_{L^{2}W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\eta\|_{L^{2}H^{3/2-\alpha}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{L^{2}H^{3/2-\alpha}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}^{2}+\varepsilon\|\partial_{t}^{2}\eta\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|u\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}u\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}^{2}+\|u\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}^{2}+\|\theta\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\theta\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}^{2}+\|\theta\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|[\partial_{t}^{2}\eta]_{\ell}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|[\partial_{t}\eta]_{\ell}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}),

where
\tilde{C}
is a small universal constant that guarantees the square root of (
4.56
) satisfying

(4.57)

d((\tilde{u}^{1},\tilde{p}^{1},\tilde{\theta}^{1},\tilde{\eta}^{1}),(\tilde{u}^{2},\tilde{p}^{2},\tilde{\theta}^{2},\tilde{\eta}^{2}))<\frac{1}{2}d((u^{1},0,\theta^{1},\eta^{1}),(u^{2},0,\theta^{2},\eta^{2})).

Here
d(\cdot,\cdot)
is the metric defined in (
4.13
). Consequently, the map
A
defined in (
4.16
) is contracted. So the Banach’s fixed point theory implies that the nonlinear
\varepsilon-
regularized system (
1.21
) with
(D_{t}^{j}u,\partial_{t}^{j}p,\partial_{t}^{j}\theta,\partial_{t}^{j}\eta)
instead of
(D_{t}^{j}u,\partial_{t}^{j}p,\partial_{t}^{j}\theta,\partial_{t}^{j}\xi)
, has a solution
(u,p,\theta,\eta)\in S(T,\delta)
for
T<T_{\varepsilon}\leq\varepsilon
.
The uniqueness for (
1.21
) with
(D_{t}^{j}u,\partial_{t}^{j}p,\partial_{t}^{j}\theta,\partial_{t}^{j}\eta)
instead of
(D_{t}^{j}u,\partial_{t}^{j}p,\partial_{t}^{j}\theta,\partial_{t}^{j}\xi)
can be proved similarly.
∎

5.
Global Well-Posedness for the Boussinesq System

5.1.
Uniform Bounds for
\varepsilon
–Regularized Solutions

We have proved the the existence and uniqueness of solutions
(u^{\varepsilon},p^{\varepsilon},\theta^{\varepsilon},\eta^{\varepsilon})
to the regularized system (
5.1
) when
\varepsilon>0
. In order to get the solutions solving (
1.11
) when
\varepsilon=0
, we have to prove that the solutions
(u^{\varepsilon},p^{\varepsilon},\theta^{\varepsilon},\eta^{\varepsilon})
obtained to (
5.1
) is uniformly bounded with respect to
\varepsilon
, and pass the limit
\varepsilon\to 0
to get the results desired.

Note that
(\partial_{t}^{j}u^{\varepsilon},\partial_{t}^{j}p^{\varepsilon},\partial_{t}^{j}\theta^{\varepsilon},\partial_{t}^{j}\eta^{\varepsilon})
,
j=0,1
and
(\partial_{t}^{2}u^{\varepsilon},\partial_{t}^{2}\theta^{\varepsilon},\partial_{t}^{2}\eta^{\varepsilon})
satisfy the equations

(5.1)
