\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{t}^{j+1}u^{\varepsilon}+\mu\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\mathcal{A}^{\varepsilon}}S_{\mathcal{A}^{\varepsilon}}(\partial_{t}^{j}p^{\varepsilon},\partial_{t}^{j}u^{\varepsilon})=g\partial_{t}^{j}\theta e_{2}+F^{1,j,\varepsilon}\quad&\text{in}&\ \Omega,\\
&\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\mathcal{A}^{\varepsilon}}\partial_{t}^{j}u^{\varepsilon}=F^{2,j,\varepsilon}\quad&\text{in}&\ \Omega,\\
&\partial_{t}^{j+1}\theta^{\varepsilon}+k\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\mathcal{A}^{\varepsilon}}\nabla_{\mathcal{A}^{\varepsilon}}\partial_{t}^{j}\theta^{\varepsilon}=F^{8,j,\varepsilon}\quad&\text{in}&\ \Omega,\\
&S_{\mathcal{A}^{\varepsilon}}(\partial_{t}^{j}p^{\varepsilon},\partial_{t}^{j}u^{\varepsilon})\mathcal{N}^{\varepsilon}=\left[g\partial_{t}^{j}\eta^{\varepsilon}-\sigma_{1}\partial_{1}\left(\frac{\partial_{1}\partial_{t}^{j}\eta+\varepsilon\partial_{t}^{j+1}\eta^{\varepsilon}}{(1+|\partial_{1}\zeta_{0}|^{2})^{3/2}}+F^{3,j,\varepsilon}\right)\right]\mathcal{N}^{\varepsilon}+F^{4,j,\varepsilon}\quad&\text{on}&\ \Sigma,\\
&\partial_{t}^{j+1}\eta^{\varepsilon}-\partial_{t}^{j}u^{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{\varepsilon}=F^{6,j,\varepsilon}\quad&\text{on}&\ \Sigma,\\
&k\nabla_{\mathcal{A}^{\varepsilon}}\partial_{t}^{j}\theta^{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{\varepsilon}+\partial_{t}^{j}\theta^{\varepsilon}=F^{9,j,\varepsilon}\quad&\text{on}&\ \Sigma,\\
&(S_{\mathcal{A}^{\varepsilon}}(\partial_{t}^{j}p^{\varepsilon},\partial_{t}^{j}u^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nu-\beta\partial_{t}^{j}u^{\varepsilon})\cdot\tau=F^{5,j,\varepsilon}\quad&\text{on}&\ \Sigma_{s},\\
&\partial_{t}^{j}u^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nu=0\quad&\text{on}&\ \Sigma_{s},\\
&\partial_{t}^{j}\theta^{\varepsilon}=0\quad&\text{on}&\ \Sigma_{s},\\
&\kappa\partial_{t}^{j+1}\eta^{\varepsilon}=\mp\sigma_{1}\left(\frac{\partial_{1}\partial_{t}^{j}\eta+\varepsilon\partial_{t}^{j+1}\eta^{\varepsilon}}{(1+|\partial_{1}\zeta_{0}|^{2})^{3/2}}+F^{3,j,\varepsilon}\right)-\kappa F^{7,j,\varepsilon}\quad&\text{on}&\ \pm\ell,\end{aligned}\right.

in the strong and weak sense respectively, where the forcing terms are given in Appendix
A
.

We now show the natural energy-dissipation estimate. We denote
\mathcal{A}^{\varepsilon}
,
\mathcal{N}^{\varepsilon}
,
J^{\varepsilon}
and
K^{\varepsilon}
are defined in terms of
\eta^{\varepsilon}
, in the same way as
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{N}
,
J
and
K
are defined in terms of
\eta
.

Theorem 5.1
.

Suppose that there exists a universal constant
\delta\in(0,1)
such that if a solution to (
5.1
) exists on the time interval
[0,T)
for
0<T\leq\infty
and obeys the estimate

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\mathcal{E}^{\varepsilon}(t)\leq\delta_{0}
,
Then there exists universal constants
C>0
and
\lambda>0
such that

(5.2)

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}e^{\lambda t}\mathcal{E}^{\varepsilon}(t)+\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{D}^{\varepsilon}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t\leq C\mathcal{E}^{\varepsilon}(0).

Proof of Theorem
5.1
.

We only give the sketch of proof for the uniform bounds of
\theta^{\varepsilon}
. Other parts can be established by the argument in
[
12
]
with the modifications to surface tension.

Step 1 – Energy-Dissipation Estimates for temperature

By the standard argument, we have
the energy-dissipation equality for
\theta^{\varepsilon}
with
j=0
in (
5.1
), and for
\partial_{t}\theta^{\varepsilon}
with
j=1
:

(5.3)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}|\theta^{\varepsilon}|^{2}J^{\varepsilon}+\frac{k}{2}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla_{\mathcal{A}^{\varepsilon}}\theta^{\varepsilon}|^{2}J^{\varepsilon}+\int_{-\ell}^{\ell}|\mathcal{N}^{\varepsilon}||\theta^{\varepsilon}|^{2}=0,

(5.4)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}|\partial_{t}\theta^{\varepsilon}|^{2}J^{\varepsilon}+\frac{k}{2}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla_{\mathcal{A}^{\varepsilon}}\partial_{t}\theta^{\varepsilon}|^{2}J^{\varepsilon}+\int_{-\ell}^{\ell}|\mathcal{N}^{\varepsilon}||\partial_{t}\theta^{\varepsilon}|^{2}=\mathcal{F}^{8,\varepsilon}(\partial_{t}u^{\varepsilon},\partial_{t}\theta^{\varepsilon},\partial_{t}\eta^{\varepsilon}),

(5.5)

\displaystyle\text{where}\ \mathcal{F}^{8,\varepsilon}(\partial_{t}u^{\varepsilon},\partial_{t}p^{\varepsilon},\partial_{t}\eta^{\varepsilon})

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}F^{8,1,\varepsilon}\cdot\partial_{t}\theta^{\varepsilon}J^{\varepsilon}-\int_{-\ell}^{\ell}F^{9,1,\varepsilon}\cdot\partial_{t}\theta^{\varepsilon}.

Then the nonlinear interaction estimates for (
5.3
) and (
5.4
) imply the energy-dissipation estimate for
\theta^{\varepsilon}
:

(5.6)

\displaystyle\|\theta^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{H^{0}}^{2}+\int_{s}^{t}\left(\|\theta^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\|u^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(\Sigma)}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\theta^{\varepsilon}(s)\|_{H^{0}}^{2},

and the energy-dissipation estimate for
\partial_{t}\theta^{\varepsilon}
:

(5.7)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}\theta^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{H^{0}}^{2}++\int_{s}^{t}\left(\|\partial_{t}\theta^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\theta^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(\Sigma)}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial_{t}\theta^{\varepsilon}(s)\|_{H^{0}}^{2}+\int_{s}^{t}\sqrt{\mathcal{E}^{\varepsilon}}\mathcal{D}^{\varepsilon},

as well as the energy-dissipation estimate for
\partial_{t}^{2}\theta^{\varepsilon}
:

(5.8)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{2}\theta^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{H^{0}}^{2}-\left(\mathcal{E}^{\varepsilon}(t)\right)^{3/2}+\int_{s}^{t}\left(\|\partial_{t}^{2}\theta^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{2}\theta^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(\Sigma)}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial_{t}^{2}\theta^{\varepsilon}(s)\|_{H^{0}}^{2}+(\mathcal{E}^{\varepsilon}(s))^{3/2}+\int_{s}^{t}\sqrt{\mathcal{E}^{\varepsilon}}\mathcal{D}^{\varepsilon}

for all
0\leq s\leq t\leq T
. We set

\mathcal{E}_{\shortparallel}^{\varepsilon}:=\sum_{j=0}^{2}\|\partial_{t}^{j}\theta^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}
,
and

\mathcal{D}_{\shortparallel}^{\varepsilon}:=\sum_{j=0}^{2}\|\partial_{t}^{j}\theta^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{j}\theta^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(\Sigma)}^{2}.

Then the inequalities (
5.6
)–(
5.8
) produce the estimate

(5.9)

\mathcal{E}_{\shortparallel}^{\varepsilon}(t)-(\mathcal{E}^{\varepsilon}(t))^{3/2}+\int_{s}^{t}\mathcal{D}_{\shortparallel}^{\varepsilon}\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{\shortparallel}^{\varepsilon}(s)+(\mathcal{E}^{\varepsilon}(s))^{3/2}+\int_{s}^{t}\sqrt{\mathcal{E}^{\varepsilon}}\mathcal{D}^{\varepsilon}

for all
0\leq s\leq t\leq T
.

Step 2 – Enhanced Dissipation and Energy Estimates

The elliptic theory for
\theta^{\varepsilon}
in (
5.1
), with
j=0,1
, tells us that

(5.10)

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}\|\theta^{\varepsilon}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}\lesssim\int_{s}^{t}\|\partial_{t}\theta^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\sqrt{\mathcal{E}^{\varepsilon}}\mathcal{D}^{\varepsilon},

and

(5.11)

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}\|\partial_{t}\theta^{\varepsilon}\|_{W^{2,q_{-}}}^{2}\lesssim\int_{s}^{t}\|\partial_{t}^{2}\theta^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\sqrt{\mathcal{E}^{\varepsilon}}\mathcal{D}^{\varepsilon},

for all
0\leq s\leq t\leq T
.

Consequently, we can get the estimate

(5.12)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\shortparallel}^{\varepsilon}(t)-(\mathcal{E}^{\varepsilon}(t))^{3/2}+\int_{s}^{t}\mathcal{D}_{\shortparallel}^{\varepsilon}+\|\theta^{\varepsilon}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\theta^{\varepsilon}\|_{W^{2,q_{-}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{\shortparallel}^{\varepsilon}(s)+(\mathcal{E}^{\varepsilon}(s))^{3/2}+\int_{s}^{t}\sqrt{\mathcal{E}^{\varepsilon}}\mathcal{D}^{\varepsilon}.

The interpolation theory allows us to enhance the energy:

(5.13)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}\theta^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{H^{1+\varepsilon_{-}/2}}^{2}\lesssim\|\partial_{t}u^{\varepsilon}(s)\|_{H^{1+\varepsilon_{-}/2}}^{2}+\int_{s}^{t}\mathcal{D}^{\varepsilon}.

The elliptic theory for the (
5.1
) with
j=0
produces the bounded estimate:

(5.14)

\displaystyle\|\theta^{\varepsilon}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}\lesssim\|\partial_{t}\theta^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\mathcal{E}^{\varepsilon}.

Thus, with the estimate in (
5.12
), we have

(5.15)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\shortparallel}^{\varepsilon}(t)+\|\theta^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}-(\mathcal{E}^{\varepsilon}(t))^{3/2}+\int_{s}^{t}(\mathcal{D}_{\shortparallel}^{\varepsilon}+\|\theta^{\varepsilon}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\theta^{\varepsilon}\|_{W^{2,q_{-}}}^{2})

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{E}^{\varepsilon}(s)+(\mathcal{E}^{\varepsilon}(s))^{3/2}+\int_{s}^{t}\sqrt{\mathcal{E}^{\varepsilon}}\mathcal{D}^{\varepsilon}.

Finally, with the argument in
[
12
]
, we combine (
5.15
) with the uniform bounds for
(u^{\varepsilon},p^{\varepsilon},\eta^{\varepsilon})
, to deduce that

(5.16)

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq t^{\prime}}\left(\mathcal{E}^{\varepsilon}(t)-(\mathcal{E}^{\varepsilon}(t))^{3/2}\right)+\int_{0}^{t^{\prime}}\mathcal{D}^{\varepsilon}\lesssim\mathcal{E}^{\varepsilon}(0)+(\mathcal{E}^{\varepsilon}(0))^{3/2}+\int_{0}^{t^{\prime}}\sqrt{\mathcal{E}^{\varepsilon}}\mathcal{D}^{\varepsilon},

for any
t^{\prime}\leq T
.

Step 3 – Synthesis

We take
s=0
and restrict
\delta
to be smaller if necessary, so that if

(5.17)

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\mathcal{E}^{\varepsilon}(t)\leq\delta\ll 1,

then we can absorb the power terms of
\mathcal{E}
in (
5.16
) to obtain the estimate

(5.18)

\sup_{0\leq t\leq t^{\prime}}\mathcal{E}^{\varepsilon}(t)+\int_{0}^{t^{\prime}}\mathcal{D}^{\varepsilon}(s)\,\mathrm{d}s\lesssim\mathcal{E}^{\varepsilon}(0),

for each
t^{\prime}\leq T
, so that

(5.19)

\mathcal{E}^{\varepsilon}(t)+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{D}^{\varepsilon}(s)\,\mathrm{d}s\lesssim\mathcal{E}^{\varepsilon}(0)

for any
t\leq T

Since
\mathcal{E}^{\varepsilon}\lesssim\mathcal{D}^{\varepsilon}
,
\mathcal{E}^{\varepsilon}
is integrable over
(0,T)
. The Gronwall’s lemma guarantees that there exists a universal constant
\lambda>0
such that

e^{\lambda t}\mathcal{E}^{\varepsilon}(t)\lesssim\mathcal{E}^{\varepsilon}(0)
,
for all
t<T
. Then the inequality (
5.19
) also implies

(5.20)

\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{D}^{\varepsilon}\lesssim\mathcal{E}^{\varepsilon}(0).

∎

5.2.
Global well-posedness of nonlinear contact points problem

In this section, we give the global existence of solutions to the original nonlinear system (
1.11
) when
\varepsilon=0
. We first prescribe the initial conditions for (
1.11
) with
\varepsilon=0
, then give the global results for the
\varepsilon
-regularized nonlinear system (
1.11
) and finally passing to the limit
\varepsilon\to 0
to complete the proof.

The initial data
(u_{0},p_{0},\theta_{0},\eta_{0},\partial_{t}u(0),\partial_{t}p(0),\partial_{t}\theta(0),\partial_{t}\eta(0),\partial_{t}^{2}u(0),\partial_{t}^{2}\theta(0),\partial_{t}^{2}\eta(0))
for (
1.11
) are assumed in the space of
\mathcal{E}
.
In addition, we assume that the compatibility conditions (
1.16
) that the initial data satisfied and that
\partial_{t}^{k}\eta(0)
,
k=0,1,2
, satisfying the zero average condition (
1.17
).

Assume the initial data
(u_{0}^{\varepsilon},p_{0}^{\varepsilon},\theta_{0}^{\varepsilon},\eta_{0}^{\varepsilon},\partial_{t}u^{\varepsilon}(0),\partial_{t}p^{\varepsilon}(0),\partial_{t}\theta^{\varepsilon}(0),\partial_{t}\eta^{\varepsilon}(0),\partial_{t}^{2}u^{\varepsilon}(0),\partial_{t}^{2}\theta^{\varepsilon}(0),\partial_{t}^{2}\eta^{\varepsilon}(0))
for the
\varepsilon
- regularized nonlinear system (
1.21
) satisfy the compatibility conditions (
1.16
), zero average conditions (
1.17
) and
\mathcal{E}^{\varepsilon}(0)<\delta
for a universal constant
\delta>0
sufficiently small and
0<\varepsilon<1
. Then the local well-posedness in Theorem
4.3
and the uniform bounds in Theorem
5.1
allow us to apply the standard continuity argument to arrive at the global well-posedness for the nonlinear system (
1.21
) with
(D_{t}^{j}u,\partial_{t}^{j}p,\partial_{t}^{j}\theta,\partial_{t}^{j}\eta)
instead of
(D_{t}^{j}u,\partial_{t}^{j}p,\partial_{t}^{j}\theta,\partial_{t}^{j}\xi)
.

Theorem 5.2
.

Suppose that the initial data
(u_{0},p_{0},\theta_{0},\eta_{0},\partial_{t}u(0),\partial_{t}p(0),\partial_{t}\theta(0),\partial_{t}\eta(0),\partial_{t}^{2}u(0),\\
\partial_{t}^{2}\theta(0),\partial_{t}^{2}\eta(0))
for (
1.21
) as well as compatibility conditions (
1.16
)
as well as zero average conditions (
1.17
). Then there exists a unique solution
(u^{\varepsilon},p^{\varepsilon},\theta^{\varepsilon},\eta^{\varepsilon})
to (
1.21
) global in time. Moreover,
(u^{\varepsilon},p^{\varepsilon},\theta^{\varepsilon},\eta^{\varepsilon})
obeys

(5.21)

\sup_{t>0}e^{\lambda t}\mathcal{E}^{\varepsilon}(t)+\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathcal{D}^{\varepsilon}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t\leq C\mathcal{E}^{\varepsilon}(0),

for universal constants
C>0
and
\lambda>0
, where
\mathcal{E}^{\varepsilon}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\varepsilon}
are defined in (
1.22
) and (
1.23
) respectively.

Consequently, we can state the global result for the original nonlinear contact points system (
1.11
) at
\varepsilon=0
.

Theorem 5.3
.

Assume that if as
\varepsilon\to 0
,
\mathcal{E}^{\varepsilon}(0)\to\mathcal{E}(0)
or specifically,

(5.22)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &(u_{0}^{\varepsilon},p_{0}^{\varepsilon},\theta_{0}^{\varepsilon},\eta_{0}^{\varepsilon},\partial_{t}u^{\varepsilon}(0),\partial_{t}p^{\varepsilon}(0),\partial_{t}\theta^{\varepsilon}(0),\partial_{t}\eta^{\varepsilon}(0),\partial_{t}^{2}u^{\varepsilon}(0),\partial_{t}^{2}\theta^{\varepsilon}(0),\partial_{t}^{2}\eta^{\varepsilon}(0),\varepsilon\partial_{t}\eta^{\varepsilon}(0),\varepsilon\partial_{t}^{2}\eta^{\varepsilon}(0),\\
&\varepsilon^{1/2}\partial_{t}^{2}\eta^{\varepsilon}(0))\to(u_{0},p_{0},\theta_{0},\eta_{0},\partial_{t}u(0),\partial_{t}p(0),\partial_{t}\theta(0)\partial_{t}\eta(0),\partial_{t}^{2}u(0),\partial_{t}^{2}\theta(0),\partial_{t}^{2}\eta(0),0,0,0),\quad\text{as}\ \varepsilon\to 0,\end{aligned}

in the space

(5.23)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} W^{2,q_{+}}(\Omega)\times W^{1,q_{+}}(\Omega)\times W^{2,q_{+}}(\Omega)\times W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}(\Sigma)\times H^{1+\varepsilon_{-}/2}(\Omega)\times H^{0}(\Omega)\times H^{1+\varepsilon_{-}/2}(\Omega)\\
\times H^{3/2+(\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha)/2}(\Sigma)\times H^{0}(\Omega)\times H^{0}(\Omega)\times H^{1}(\Sigma)\times W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}(\Sigma)\times W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}(\Sigma)\times H^{3/2-\alpha}(\Sigma).\end{aligned}

Then
(u^{\varepsilon},p^{\varepsilon},\theta^{\varepsilon},\eta^{\varepsilon})
converges to the limit
(u,p,\theta,\eta)
that is the unique solution to the original nonlinear system (
1.11
) at
\varepsilon=0
with initial data stated in Theorem
1.1
. The limit
(u,p,\theta,\eta)
obeys the estimate

(5.24)

\sup_{t>0}e^{\lambda t}\mathcal{E}(t)+\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathcal{D}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t\leq C\mathcal{E}(0),

for universal constants
C>0
and
\lambda>0
, where
\mathcal{E}
and
\mathcal{D}
are defined in (
1.14
) and (
1.15
) respectively.

We note that Theorem
5.3
is conditional. In order to let Theorem
5.3
make sense, we have to prove that there really exist the initial data satisfying the limiting conditions in Theorem
5.3
. Thanks to Theorem
B.1
, the one more term
\partial_{t}^{2}\eta(0)\in W^{2-1/q_{+},q_{+}}(\Sigma)
beyond the initial energy
\mathcal{E}(0)
can guarantee that these initial data do exist and obey the conditions in Appendix
B.2
. Consequently, it is sufficient to complete the global well-posedness in Theorem
1.1
.

Appendix A
Forcing Terms in
\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\mathcal{A}}
–Free System

We now present the nonlinear interaction terms in (
1.19
) and (
1.20
),
j=0,1,2
. From (
1.11
), we directly set

(A.1)

F^{8}=\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}WK\partial_{2}\theta-u\cdot\nabla_{\mathcal{A}}\theta,\quad F^{9}=0.

\displaystyle F^{1}=\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}WK\partial_{2}u-u\cdot\nabla_{\mathcal{A}}u-g\theta e_{2},\quad F^{3}=\sigma_{1}\mathcal{R}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta),\quad F^{4}=\sigma_{2}\theta\partial_{1}\left(\frac{\partial_{1}\eta+\varepsilon\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\eta}{(1+|\partial_{1}\eta|)^{1/2}}\right)\mathcal{N},

\displaystyle F^{5}=0,\quad F^{7}=\kappa\hat{\mathscr{W}}(\partial_{t}\eta),\quad F^{8}=\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}WK\partial_{2}\theta-u\cdot\nabla_{\mathcal{A}}\theta,\quad F^{9}=0.

Then for
j=1
, the forcing terms are given via

(A.2)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} F^{8,1}&=\partial_{t}F^{8}+k\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}}\nabla_{\mathcal{A}}\theta+k\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\mathcal{A}}\nabla_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}}\theta,\\
F^{9,1}&=-k\nabla_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}}\theta\cdot\mathcal{N}-k\nabla_{\mathcal{A}}\theta\cdot\partial_{t}\mathcal{N}-\theta\partial_{t}|\mathcal{N}|.\end{aligned}

\displaystyle F^{1,1}

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}F^{1}-\partial_{t}Ru-RD_{t}u-R^{2}u-\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}}S_{\mathcal{A}}(p,u)+\mu\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\mathcal{A}}\mathbb{D}_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}}u+\mu\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\mathcal{A}}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{A}}(Ru),

\displaystyle F^{3,1}

\displaystyle=\sigma_{1}\partial_{t}\mathcal{R}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta),

\displaystyle F^{4,1}

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}F^{4}+\left[g(\eta+\varepsilon\partial_{t}\eta)-\sigma_{1}\partial_{1}\left(\frac{\partial_{1}\eta+\varepsilon\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\eta}{(1+|\partial_{1}\zeta_{0}|)^{3/2}}+F^{3}\right)\right]\partial_{t}\mathcal{N}-S_{\mathcal{A}}(p,u)\partial_{t}\mathcal{N}

\displaystyle+\mu\mathbb{D}_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}}u\mathcal{N}+\mu\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{A}}(Ru)\mathcal{N},

\displaystyle F^{5,1}

\displaystyle=\mu\mathbb{D}_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}}u\nu\cdot\tau+\mu\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{A}}(Ru)\nu\cdot\tau,

\displaystyle F^{7,1}

\displaystyle=\kappa\hat{\mathscr{W}}^{\prime}(\partial_{t}\eta)\partial_{t}^{2}\eta.

The forcing terms for
j=2
are given via

(A.3)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} F^{8,2}&=\partial_{t}F^{8,1}+k\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}}\nabla_{\mathcal{A}}\partial_{t}\theta+k\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\mathcal{A}}\nabla_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}}\partial_{t}\theta,\\
F^{9,2}&=\partial_{t}F^{9,1}-k\nabla_{\mathcal{A}}\partial_{t}\theta\cdot\partial_{t}\mathcal{N}-k\nabla_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}}\partial_{t}\theta\cdot\mathcal{N}-\partial_{t}\theta\partial_{t}|\mathcal{N}|.\end{aligned}

\displaystyle F^{1,2}

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}F^{1,1}-\partial_{t}RD_{t}u-RD_{t}^{2}u-R^{2}D_{t}u-\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}}S_{\mathcal{A}}(\partial_{t}p,D_{t}u)+\mu\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\mathcal{A}}\mathbb{D}_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}}D_{t}u

\displaystyle+\mu\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\mathcal{A}}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{A}}(RD_{t}u),

\displaystyle F^{3,2}

\displaystyle=\sigma\partial_{t}^{2}\mathcal{R}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta),

\displaystyle F^{4,2}

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}F^{4,1}+\left[g(\partial_{t}\eta+\varepsilon\partial_{t}^{2}\eta)-\sigma_{1}\partial_{1}\left(\frac{\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\eta+\varepsilon\partial_{1}\partial_{t}^{2}\eta}{(1+|\partial_{1}\zeta_{0}|)^{3/2}}+\partial_{t}F^{3}\right)\right]\partial_{t}\mathcal{N}

\displaystyle-S_{\mathcal{A}}(\partial_{t}p,D_{t}u)\partial_{t}\mathcal{N}+\mu\mathbb{D}_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}}D_{t}u\mathcal{N}+\mu\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{A}}(RD_{t}u)\mathcal{N},

\displaystyle F^{5,2}

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}F^{5,1}+\mu\mathbb{D}_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}}D_{t}u\nu\cdot\tau+\mu\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{A}}(RD_{t}u)\nu\cdot\tau,

\displaystyle F^{7,2}

\displaystyle=\kappa\hat{\mathscr{W}}^{\prime}(\partial_{t}\eta)\partial_{t}^{3}\eta+\kappa\hat{\mathscr{W}}^{\prime\prime}(\partial_{t}\eta)(\partial_{t}^{2}\eta)^{2}.

Then we present the forcing terms in (
5.1
). For simplicity, we can directly denote
F^{i,j,\varepsilon}=F^{i,j}(u^{\varepsilon},p^{\varepsilon},\theta^{\varepsilon},\eta^{\varepsilon})
,
i=1,3,4,5,7,8,9
and
j=0,1,2
. That means
F^{i,j,\varepsilon}
is actually
F^{i,j}
with
(u,p,\theta,\eta)
replaced by
(u^{\varepsilon},p^{\varepsilon},\theta^{\varepsilon},\eta^{\varepsilon})
.

Appendix B
Initial Data for
\varepsilon
–Regularized Nonlinear Problem

In the process of constructing solutions to (
3.43
) by the Galerkin method, one necessary ingredient is to ensure that the initial data of the sequence of approximate solutions converge to the initial data of PDE (
3.43
).
Now we state our theorem for the construction of initial conditions for nonlinear system (
1.11
).

Theorem B.1
.

Suppose that the initial data
\partial_{t}^{2}\eta(0)\in\mathring{H}^{1}(\Sigma)\cap W^{2-1/q_{+},q_{+}}(\Sigma)
,
\partial_{t}^{2}\theta(0)\in H^{0}(\Omega)
and
D_{t}^{2}u(0)\in H^{0}(\Omega)
are given and satisfy
\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}D_{t}^{2}u(0)=0
. Then we can construct
(u_{0},p_{0},\theta_{0},\eta_{0})
and
(\partial_{t}u(0),\partial_{t}p(0),\partial_{t}\theta(0),\partial_{t}\eta(0))

solving the system (
1.11
) at
t=0
such that

(B.1)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{\varepsilon}(0)+\|\partial_{t}^{2}\eta^{\varepsilon}(0)\|_{W^{2-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}\leq\delta

for a universal constant
\delta>0
sufficiently small provided that

(B.2)

\displaystyle\|D_{t}^{2}u(0)\|_{H^{0}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{2}\theta(0)\|_{H^{0}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{2}\eta(0)\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{2}\eta(0)\|_{W^{2-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}\leq\delta_{0}

for a universal constant
\delta_{0}>0
sufficiently small.

Remark B.1
.

It is important to note that the initial data in Theorem
B.1
implies the existence of
\mathcal{E}^{\varepsilon}(0)
with
\mathcal{E}^{\varepsilon}
defined in (
1.22
). So Theorem
B.1
guarantees that our initial data for the nonlinear system (
1.11
) are well defined.

B.1.
Initial Data for Linear heat PDEs

Our aim is to use the Galerkin method to give the existence and uniqueness of solutions to (
1.19
). So, the important thing is to ensure that the initial data of the sequence of approximate solutions converge to the initial data of PDE (
1.19
). We assume
the forcing terms

(B.3)

F^{8}(0),\ \partial_{t}F^{8}(0)\in L^{q_{+}}(\Omega),\quad F^{9}(0),\ \partial_{t}F^{9}(0)\in W^{1-1/q_{+},q_{+}}(\Sigma)

and
\mathcal{A}_{0}
,
\mathcal{N}(0)
,
J(0)=1/K(0)
are given in terms of
\eta_{0}
.
The construction of the initial data are presented in Lemma
B.3
.

We first prescribe the initial data of higher order
\partial_{t}^{2}\theta(0)
, then construct the initial data of lower order
\partial_{t}\theta(0)
and
\theta_{0}
. This is a backward procedure for time regularity.
\partial_{t}^{2}\theta(0)\in H^{0}(\Omega)
is natural for the
dynamical energy of the linear and nonlinear system for
t>0
.

We now use elliptic theory to construct
\partial_{t}\theta(0)
. First, it is necessary to
assume the initial data
F^{8,1}(0)
and
F^{9,1}(0)
are defined as

(B.4)

\displaystyle F^{8,1}:=\partial_{t}F^{8}+\mathfrak{G}^{8}(\theta),\quad F^{9,1}:=\partial_{t}F^{9}+\mathfrak{G}^{9}(\theta),

where

(B.5)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{G}^{8}(\theta)=k\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\mathcal{A}}\nabla_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}}\theta+k\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{A}}\theta,\ \mathfrak{G}^{9}(\theta)=-k\nabla_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}}\theta\cdot\mathcal{N}-k\nabla_{\mathcal{A}}\theta\cdot\partial_{t}\mathcal{N}-\theta\partial_{t}|\mathcal{N}|.

We define

(B.6)

F^{8,2}:=\mathfrak{G}^{8}(\partial_{t}\theta)+\partial_{t}F^{8,1},\ F^{9,2}:=\mathfrak{G}^{9}(\partial_{t}\theta)+\partial_{t}F^{9,1}.

Then we have the following lemma.

Lemma B.2
.

F^{8,1}(0)\in L^{q_{+}}(\Omega),\ F^{9,1}(0)\in W^{1-1/q_{+},q_{+}}(\Sigma)
T are implied by the following estimates

(B.7)

\|F^{8,1}(0)\|_{L^{q_{+}}(\Omega)}\lesssim\|\partial_{t}F^{8}(0)\|_{L^{q_{+}}}+\|\partial_{t}\eta(0)\|_{H^{3/2+(\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha)/2}}\|\theta_{0}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}},

(B.8)

\displaystyle\|F^{9,1}(0)\|_{W^{1-1/q_{+},q_{+}}(\Sigma)}\lesssim\|\partial_{t}F^{9}(0)\|_{W^{1-1/q_{+},q_{+}}(\Sigma)}+\|\partial_{t}\eta(0)\|_{H^{3/2+(\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha)/2}}\|\theta_{0}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}.

Proof.

We estimate
\mathfrak{G}^{8}(\theta_{0})
term by term. By
\|\mathcal{A}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\lesssim 1
, we have

(B.9)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}J(0)K(0)\Delta_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\theta_{0}\|_{L^{q_{+}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial_{t}J(0)K(0)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}(\|\nabla^{2}\theta_{0}\|_{L^{q_{+}}}+\|\nabla\mathcal{A}_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\theta_{0}\|_{L^{2/(1-\varepsilon_{+})}})

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial_{t}\eta(0)\|_{H^{3/2+(\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha)/2}}(1+\|\eta_{0}\|_{W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}})\|\theta_{0}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}.

Similarly, we have

(B.10)

\displaystyle\|\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\nabla_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}(0)}\theta_{0}+\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}(0)}\nabla_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\theta_{0}\|_{L^{q_{+}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}(0)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\nabla^{2}\theta_{0}\|_{L^{q_{+}}}+\|\nabla\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}(0)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\theta_{0}\|_{L^{2/(1-\varepsilon_{+})}}

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}(0)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\nabla^{2}\theta_{0}\|_{L^{q_{+}}}+\|\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}(0)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\mathcal{A}_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\theta_{0}\|_{L^{2/(1-\varepsilon_{+})}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial_{t}\eta(0)\|_{H^{3/2+(\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha)/2}}(1+\|\eta_{0}\|_{W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}})\|\theta_{0}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}.

The combination of (
B.9
)–(
B.10
) gives the bound of (
B.7
).

We turn to the bounds for
\mathfrak{G}^{9}(\theta_{0})
. We first employ Sobolev embedding theory and usual trace theory to deduce that

(B.11)

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}(0)}\theta_{0}\cdot\mathcal{N}(0)\|_{W^{1-1/q_{+},q_{+}}(\Sigma)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}(0)}\theta_{0}\|_{W^{1,q_{+}}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}(0)}\theta_{0}\|_{L^{q_{+}}}+\|\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}(0)\nabla\theta_{0})\|_{L^{q_{+}}}

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}(0)\nabla^{2}\theta_{0})\|_{L^{q_{+}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial_{t}\eta(0)\|_{H^{3/2+(\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha)/2}}\|\theta_{0}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}.

The similar argument allows us to deduce

(B.12)

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\theta_{0}\partial_{t}\mathcal{N}(0)\|_{W^{1-1/q_{+},q_{+}}(\Sigma)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\theta_{0}\|_{W^{1,q_{+}}(\Omega)}(\|\partial_{t}\mathcal{N}(0)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\partial_{t}\mathcal{N}(0)\|_{W^{1,q_{+}}(\Sigma)})

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla\theta_{0}\|_{W^{1,q_{+}}}+\||\nabla\mathcal{A}_{0}|\nabla\theta_{0}\|_{L^{q_{+}}})\|\partial_{t}\eta(0)\|_{H^{3/2+(\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha)/2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial_{t}\eta(0)\|_{H^{3/2+(\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha)/2}}\|\theta_{0}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}},

and

(B.13)

\|\theta_{0}\partial_{t}|\mathcal{N}(0)|\|_{W^{1-1/q_{+},q_{+}}(\Sigma)}\lesssim\|\partial_{t}\eta(0)\|_{H^{3/2+(\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha)/2}}\|\theta_{0}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}.

The estimates (
B.11
)–(
B.13
) along with the assumption
\partial_{t}F^{9}(0)\in W^{1-1/q_{+},q_{+}}(\Sigma)
imply (
B.8
).

∎

Under the assumption on
\partial_{t}^{2}\theta(0)
and Lemma
B.2
, we wish to construct
\partial_{t}\theta(0)
. However, from Lemma
B.2
, we know that the forcing terms depend on
\theta_{0}
, whichh is yet unknown. So we need an iteration as follows.

(B.14)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &-k\Delta_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\partial_{t}\theta^{n+1}(0)=\partial_{t}^{2}\theta(0)+F^{8,1}(\theta_{0}^{n})\quad&\text{in}&\ \Omega,\\
&k\nabla_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\partial_{t}\theta^{n+1}(0)\cdot\mathcal{N}(0)+\partial_{t}\theta^{n+1}(0)|\mathcal{N}(0)|=F^{9,1}(\theta_{0}^{n})\quad&\text{on}&\ \Sigma,\\
&\partial_{t}\theta^{n+1}(0)=0\quad&\text{on}&\ \Sigma_{s},\end{aligned}\right.

and

(B.15)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &-k\Delta_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\theta^{n+1}(0)=\partial_{t}\theta^{n}(0)+F^{8}(0)\quad&\text{in}&\ \Omega,\\
&k\nabla_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\theta^{n+1}(0)\cdot\mathcal{N}(0)+\theta^{n+1}(0)|\mathcal{N}(0)|=F^{9}(0)\quad&\text{on}&\ \Sigma,\\
&\theta^{n+1}(0)=0\quad&\text{on}&\ \Sigma_{s}.\end{aligned}\right.

These two coupled iterations can present the construction of both
\partial_{t}\theta(0)
and
\theta_{0}
, which obey the elliptic estimates:

Lemma B.3
.

(B.16)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\|\theta_{0}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\theta(0)\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\theta(0)\|_{H^{1+\varepsilon_{-}/2}}^{2}\\
&\lesssim\|\partial_{t}^{2}\theta(0)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|F^{8}(0)\|_{L^{q_{+}}}^{2}+\|F^{9}(0)\|_{W^{1-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}+\\
&\quad+\|\partial_{t}F^{8}(0)\|_{L^{q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}F^{9}(0)\|_{W^{1-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}.\end{aligned}

Proof.

The elliptic theory to (
B.14
) allows us to construct
\partial_{t}\theta^{n+1}(0)
such that

(B.17)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}\theta^{n+1}(0)\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\theta^{n+1}(0)\|_{H^{1+\varepsilon_{-}/2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial_{t}\eta(0)\|_{H^{3/2+(\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha)/2}}^{2}\|\theta_{0}^{n}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{2}\theta(0)\|_{H^{0}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{t}F^{8}(0)\|_{L^{q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}F^{9}(0)\|_{W^{1-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}.

and to (
B.15
) allows us to construct
\theta^{n+1}_{n}
such that

(B.18)

\displaystyle\|\theta^{n+1}(0)\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}\lesssim\|\partial_{t}\theta^{n}(0)\|_{H^{0}}^{2}+\|F^{8}(0)\|_{L^{q_{+}}}^{2}+\|F^{9}(0)\|_{W^{1-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}.

by a standard contraction argument, we can show that
(\partial_{t}\theta^{n}(0),\theta_{0}^{n})\in H^{1}(\Omega)\times H^{1}(\Omega)
is a Cauchy sequence. Hence, by passing the limit
n\to\infty
,

(\partial_{t}\theta^{n}(0),\theta_{0}^{n})\times\left(W^{2,q_{+}}(\Omega)\cap H^{1+\varepsilon_{-}/2}\right)\times W^{2,q_{+}}(\Omega)
obeys the estimate (
B.16
).
∎

B.2.
Contraction and Initial Data for Nonlinear PDEs

In this section, we give the construction of the initial data for
\varepsilon
regularized nonlinear system (
1.11
) and (
1.12
). In this section, all the
(u,p,\theta,\eta)
and
(v,q,\vartheta,\xi)
should be considered with superscript
\varepsilon
. But for simplicity, we ignore
\varepsilon
.

The main result in this section is to show Theorem
B.1
by the fixed point theory. We first assume that
(u_{0},p_{0},\theta_{0},\eta_{0})
,
(D_{t}u(0),\partial_{t}p(0),\partial_{t}\theta(0),\partial_{t}\eta(0))
, and
(D_{t}^{2}u(0),\partial_{t}^{2}\theta(0),\partial_{t}^{2}\eta(0))
are given so that

\mathcal{E}^{\varepsilon}(u,p,\theta,\eta)(0)+\|\partial_{t}\eta(0)\|_{W^{2-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}\leq\delta
for
\delta
sufficiently small. Then we denote the unknowns
(v_{0},q_{0},\vartheta_{0},\xi_{0})
in (
1.20
) and (
1.19
) with
j=0
at
t=0
instead of
(u_{0},p_{0},\theta_{0},\xi_{0})
, and the unknowns
(D_{t}v(0),\partial_{t}q(0),\partial_{t}\vartheta(0),\partial_{t}\xi(0))
in (
1.20
) and (
1.19
) with
j=1
at
t=0
instead of
(D_{t}u(0),\partial_{t}p(0),\partial_{t}\theta(0),\partial_{t}\eta(0))
, and obtain the boundedness
\mathcal{E}^{\varepsilon}(v,q,\vartheta,\xi)(0)+\|\partial_{t}^{2}\xi(0)\|_{W^{2-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}\leq\delta
for nonlinear initial data, subjected to the assumptions that
D_{t}^{2}v(0)=D_{t}^{2}u(0)
,
\partial_{t}^{2}\vartheta(0)=\partial_{t}^{2}\theta(0)
and
\partial_{t}^{2}\xi(0)=\partial_{t}^{2}\eta(0)
. Finally we use the contraction to show the existence of fixed points.

In order to use Lemma
B.3
, we give the estimate of forcing terms in (
B.16
).

Lemma B.4
.

It holds that

(B.19)

\displaystyle\|F^{8}(0)\|_{L^{q_{+}}}\lesssim(\|\partial_{t}\eta(0)\|_{H^{3/2+(\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha)/2}}+\|u_{0}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2})\|\theta_{0}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}},

(B.20)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}F^{8}(0)\|_{L^{q_{+}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\|\partial_{t}^{2}\eta(0)\|_{H^{1}}+\|u_{0}\|_{H^{1}}\|\partial_{t}\eta(0)\|_{H^{3/2+(\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha)/2}}+\|D_{t}u(0)\|_{H^{1}})\|\theta_{0}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}

\displaystyle+\|u_{0}\|_{H^{1}}\|\partial_{t}\theta(0)\|_{H^{1+\varepsilon_{-}/2}}.

Proof.

From the definition of
F^{8}(0)
, we directly use the Hölder inequality to show that

(B.21)

\displaystyle\|F^{8}(0)\|_{L^{q_{+}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}(0)\partial_{2}\theta_{0}\|_{L^{q_{+}}}+\|u_{0}\cdot\nabla_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\theta_{0}\|_{L^{q_{+}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\|\partial_{t}\eta(0)\|_{H^{3/2+(\varepsilon_{-}\alpha)/2}}+\|u_{0}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}})\|\theta_{0}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}},

where in the last inequality, we have used the fact
\|\mathcal{A}_{0}\|\lesssim 1
, and the Sobolev embedding theory and usual trace theory.

We estimate
\partial_{t}F^{8}(0)
term by term. First, by the Hölder inequality, and Sobolev embedding theory and usual trace theory, we have that

(B.22)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{2}\bar{\eta}(0)K(0)\partial_{2}\theta_{0}\|_{L^{q_{+}}}\lesssim\|\partial_{t}^{2}\bar{\eta}(0)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial_{2}\theta_{0}\|_{L^{q_{+}}}\lesssim\|\partial_{t}^{2}\eta(0)\|_{H^{1}}\|\theta_{0}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}},

where we have used
\|K(0)\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 1
. Similarly,

(B.23)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}(0)\partial_{t}K(0)\partial_{2}\theta_{0}+\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}(0)K(0)\partial_{2}\partial_{t}\theta(0)\|_{L^{q_{+}}}\lesssim\|\partial_{t}\eta(0)\|_{H^{1}}(\|\theta_{0}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}+\|\partial_{t}\theta(0)\|_{H^{1+\varepsilon_{-}/2}}).

Then the lower order terms are bounded by

(B.24)

\displaystyle\|D_{t}u(0)\nabla_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\theta_{0}\|_{L^{q_{+}}}+\|u_{0}\nabla_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}(0)}\theta_{0}\|_{L^{q_{+}}}+\|u_{0}\nabla_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\partial_{t}\theta(0)\|_{L^{q_{+}}}+\|(R(0)u_{0})\nabla_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\theta_{0}\|_{L^{q_{+}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\|D_{t}u(0)\|_{H^{1}}+\|u_{0}\|_{H^{1}}\|\partial_{t}\eta(0)\|_{H^{3/2+(\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha)/2}})\|\theta_{0}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}+\|u_{0}\|_{H^{1}}\|\partial_{t}\theta(0)\|_{H^{1+\varepsilon_{-}/2}}.

So all the above estimates from (
B.22
)–(
B.24
) show the estimate (
B.20
).
∎

In order to prove the contraction, we refer to velocities as
v^{i},u^{i}
, pressures as
q^{i},p^{i}
, temperatures as
\vartheta^{i},\theta^{i}
and surface functions as
\eta^{i},\xi^{i}
,
i=1,2
. We suppose that
(u_{0}^{j},p_{0}^{j},\theta_{0}^{j},\eta_{0}^{j},D_{t}u^{j}(0),\partial_{t}p^{j}(0),\partial_{t}\eta^{j}(0))
for
j=1,2
,
as well as
(D_{t}^{2}v^{1}(0),\partial_{t}^{2}\theta^{1}(0),\partial_{t}^{2}\eta^{1}(0))=(D_{t}^{2}v^{2}(0),\partial_{t}^{2}\theta^{2}(0),\partial_{t}^{2}\eta^{2}(0))

are two initial data for the nonlinear
\varepsilon-
regularized system (
1.20
) and (
1.19
) with
\xi
replaced by
\eta
. Then it is concerned with the following systems with
j=0,1
:

(B.25)

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{t}^{j+1}\vartheta^{i}(0)-k\Delta_{\mathcal{A}^{i}_{0}}\partial_{t}^{j}\vartheta^{i}(0)=\mathfrak{f}^{8,i}_{j}(0)\quad&\text{in}&\quad\Omega,\\
&k\mathcal{N}^{i}(0)\cdot\nabla_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\partial_{t}^{j}\vartheta^{i}(0)+\vartheta|\mathcal{N}^{i}(0)|=\mathfrak{f}^{9,i}_{j}(0)\quad&\text{on}&\quad\Sigma,\\
&\partial_{t}^{j}\vartheta^{i}(0)=0\quad&\text{on}&\quad\Sigma_{s},\end{aligned}\right.

where
\mathfrak{f}^{k,i}_{j}(0)=\partial_{t}\partial_{t}F^{k}(\theta_{0}^{i},\eta_{0}^{i},\partial_{t}\eta^{i}(0))+\mathfrak{G}^{k}(\theta_{0}^{i})
,
k=8,9
,
i=1,2
, and

(B.26)

\left\{\begin{aligned} &(D_{t}^{j+1}v^{i})(0)+\nabla_{\mathcal{A}^{i}_{0}}(\partial_{t}^{j}q^{i})(0)-\mu\Delta_{\mathcal{A}^{i}_{0}}(D_{t}^{j}v^{i}(0)+2^{j}R^{i}(0)D_{t}^{j}v^{i}(0)\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad-g\partial_{t}^{j}\theta^{i}(0)e_{2}=\mathfrak{f}^{1,i}_{j}(0)\quad&\text{in}&\quad\Omega,\\
&\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\mathcal{A}^{i}_{0}}(D_{t}^{j}v^{i})(0)=0\quad&\text{in}&\quad\Omega,\\
&S_{\mathcal{A}^{i}_{0}}(\partial_{t}^{j}q^{i}(0),D_{t}^{j}v^{i}(0))\mathcal{N}^{i}(0)=\bigg[g(\partial_{t}^{j}\xi^{i}(0)+\varepsilon\partial_{t}^{j+1}\xi^{i}(0))\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad-\sigma_{1}\partial_{1}\left(\frac{\partial_{1}\partial_{t}^{j}\xi^{i}(0)+\varepsilon\partial_{1}\partial_{t}^{j+1}\xi^{i}(0)}{(1+|\partial_{1}\zeta_{0}|^{2})^{3/2}}\right)-\partial_{1}\mathfrak{f}^{3,i}_{j}(0)\bigg]\mathcal{N}(0)+\mathfrak{f}^{4,i}_{j}(0)\quad&\text{on}&\quad\Sigma,\\
&D_{t}^{j}v^{i}(0)\cdot\mathcal{N}^{i}(0)=\partial_{t}^{j+1}\xi^{i}(0)\quad&\text{on}&\quad\Sigma,\\
&(S_{\mathcal{A}^{i}_{0}}(\partial_{t}^{j}q^{i}(0),D_{t}^{j}v^{i}(0))\nu-\beta D_{t}^{j}v^{i}(0))\cdot\tau=\mathfrak{f}^{5,i}_{j}(0)\quad&\text{on}&\quad\Sigma_{s},\\
&D_{t}^{j}v^{i}(0)\cdot\nu=0\quad&\text{on}&\quad\Sigma_{s},\\
&\mp\sigma_{1}\frac{\partial_{1}\partial_{t}^{j}\xi^{i}(0)+\varepsilon\partial_{1}\partial_{t}^{j+1}\xi^{i}(0)}{(1+|\partial_{1}\zeta_{0}|^{2})^{3/2}}(\pm\ell)=\kappa(D_{t}^{j}v^{i}(0)\cdot\mathcal{N}^{i}(0))(\pm\ell)\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\pm\mathfrak{f}^{3,i}_{j}(\pm\ell,0)-\mathfrak{f}^{7,i}_{j}(\pm\ell,0),\end{aligned}\right.

where
\mathfrak{f}^{k,i}_{0}
for
i=1,2
,
k=1,3,4,5,9
can be given in
[
12
]
. We define
\mathcal{A}^{i}
,
\mathcal{N}^{i}
, etc, in terms of
\eta^{i}
,
i=1,2
. Set

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{0}

\displaystyle=\|D_{t}u^{i}(0)\|_{H^{1+\varepsilon_{-}/2}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}p^{i}(0)\|_{H^{\varepsilon_{-}/2}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\theta^{i}(0)\|_{H^{1+\varepsilon_{-}/2}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\xi^{i}(0)\|_{W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|u_{0}^{i}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|p_{0}^{i}\|_{W^{1,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\theta_{0}^{i}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\xi_{0}^{i}\|_{W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}.

We use Lemma
B.3
and
B.4
to get that

Lemma B.5
.

The solutions
\partial_{t}^{j}\vartheta(0)
solving (
B.25
) are bounded by

(B.27)

\|\vartheta_{0}^{i}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\vartheta^{i}(0)\|_{H^{1+\varepsilon_{-}/2}}^{2}\lesssim\|\partial_{t}^{2}\theta(0)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{0}.

Therefore, by assuming
\|D_{t}^{2}u(0)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{2}\theta(0)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{2}\eta(0)\|_{W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}\lesssim\delta_{0}
, we have
\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{0}\lesssim\delta

for a universal constant
\delta
sufficiently small,
i=1,2
.

For simplicity, we might abuse the notation to denote

\displaystyle u_{0}=u_{0}^{1}-u_{0}^{2},\ p_{0}=p_{0}^{1}-p_{0}^{2},\ \theta_{0}=\theta_{0}^{1}-\theta_{0}^{2},\ \xi_{0}=\xi_{0}^{1}-\xi_{0}^{2},\ v_{0}=v_{0}^{1}-v_{0}^{2},

\displaystyle q_{0}=q_{0}^{1}-q_{0}^{2},\ \vartheta_{0}=\vartheta_{0}^{1}-\vartheta_{0}^{2},\ \eta_{0}=\eta_{0}^{1}-\eta_{0}^{2},

\displaystyle D_{t}u(0)=D_{t}u^{1}(0)-D_{t}u^{2}(0),\ \partial_{t}p(0)=\partial_{t}p^{1}(0)-\partial_{t}p^{2}(0),\ \partial_{t}\theta(0)=\partial_{t}\theta^{1}(0)-\partial_{t}\theta^{2}(0),

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\xi(0)=\partial_{t}\xi^{1}(0)-\partial_{t}\xi^{2}(0),\ D_{t}v(0)=D_{t}v^{1}(0)-D_{t}v^{2}(0),\ \partial_{t}q=\partial_{t}q^{1}(0)-\partial_{t}q^{2}(0),

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\vartheta(0)=\partial_{t}\vartheta^{1}(0)-\partial_{t}\vartheta^{2}(0),\ \partial_{t}\eta(0)=\partial_{t}\eta^{1}(0)-\partial_{t}\eta^{2}(0).

Lemma B.6
.

(D_{t}v(0),\partial_{t}q(0),\partial_{t}\vartheta(0),\partial_{t}\xi(0))
obey the estimate

(B.28)

\displaystyle\|D_{t}v(0)\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}q(0)\|_{H^{0}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\vartheta(0)\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\xi(0)\|_{H^{3/2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\delta(\|D_{t}u(0)\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\|u_{0}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\theta(0)\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\|\theta_{0}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\eta_{0}\|_{W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{t}\eta(0)\|_{H^{3/2}}^{2}(\|v_{0}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\vartheta_{0}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2})+\|q_{0}\|_{W^{1,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\xi_{0}\|_{W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2})

and
(v_{0},q_{0},\theta_{0},\xi_{0})
obey the estimate

(B.29)

\displaystyle\|v_{0}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|q_{0}\|_{W^{1,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\vartheta_{0}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\xi_{0}\|_{W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left[\|D_{t}v(0)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\vartheta(0)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\delta(\|\eta_{0}\|_{W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\eta(0)\|_{H^{3/2}}^{2}+\|u_{0}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\theta_{0}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2})\right],

where
C
is a universal constant to be changed from line to line.

Proof.

We first consider the temperature.
By
\partial_{t}^{2}\vartheta^{1}(0)=\partial_{t}^{2}\vartheta^{2}(0)
,
\partial_{t}\vartheta(0)
satisfies the equations

(B.30)

\left\{\begin{aligned} &-k\Delta_{\mathcal{A}_{0}^{1}}\partial_{t}\vartheta(0)=\mathfrak{g}^{8}_{1}\quad&\text{in}&\ \Omega,\\
&k\nabla_{\mathcal{A}_{0}^{1}}\partial_{t}\vartheta(0)\cdot\mathcal{N}^{1}(0)+\partial_{t}\vartheta(0)|\mathcal{N}^{1}(0)|=\mathfrak{g}^{9}_{1}&\text{on}&\ \Sigma,\\
&\partial_{t}\vartheta(0)=0&\text{on}&\ \Sigma_{s},\end{aligned}\right.

where the forcing terms
\mathfrak{g}^{k}_{1}
,
k=8,9
, are presented in Appendix
B.3
.

We multiply the first equation of (
B.30
) by
J^{1}(0)\partial_{t}\vartheta(0)
, and then integrate over
\Omega
and integrate by parts to deduce that

\displaystyle\frac{k}{2}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla_{\mathcal{A}^{1}_{0}}\partial_{t}\vartheta(0)|^{2}J^{1}(0)+\int_{\Sigma}|\partial_{t}\vartheta(0)|^{2}|\mathcal{N}^{1}(0)|

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\mathfrak{g}^{8}_{1}\cdot\partial_{t}\vartheta(0)J^{1}(0)+\int_{\Sigma}\mathfrak{g}^{9}_{1}\partial_{t}\vartheta(0).

By the smallness of
\delta_{0}
and integration by parts, we employ the same arguments as in the proof of Lemma
B.3
to obtain the bounds

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Omega}\mathfrak{g}^{8}_{1}\cdot\partial_{t}\vartheta(0)J^{1}(0)+\int_{\Sigma}\mathfrak{g}^{9}_{1}\partial_{t}\vartheta(0)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta^{1/2}(\|\partial_{t}\eta(0)\|_{H^{3/2-\alpha}}+\|\eta_{0}\|_{W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}+\|D_{t}u(0)\|_{H^{1}}+\|u_{0}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}

\displaystyle+\|\theta_{0}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}+\|\vartheta_{0}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}})\|\partial_{t}\vartheta(0)\|_{H^{1}}.

By the Cauchy inequality, we have the bounds

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}\vartheta(0)\|_{H^{1}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\delta(\|D_{t}u(0)\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\|u_{0}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\theta_{0}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\eta_{0}\|_{W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\eta(0)\|_{H^{3/2-\alpha}}^{2})

\displaystyle+C\delta\|\vartheta_{0}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}.

\vartheta_{0}
satisfies the equations

(B.31)

\left\{\begin{aligned} &-k\Delta_{\mathcal{A}_{0}^{1}}\vartheta_{0}=\mathfrak{g}^{8}\quad&\text{in}&\ \Omega,\\
&k\nabla_{\mathcal{A}_{0}^{1}}\vartheta_{0}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{1}(0)+\vartheta_{0}|\mathcal{N}^{1}(0)|=\mathfrak{g}^{9}&\text{on}&\ \Sigma,\\
&\vartheta_{0}=0&\text{on}&\ \Sigma_{s}.\end{aligned}\right.

We directly use the elliptic estimate to get that

\displaystyle\|\vartheta_{0}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}\lesssim\|\partial_{t}\vartheta(0)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\delta(\|\eta_{0}\|_{W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\eta(0)\|_{H^{3/2-\alpha}}^{2}+\|u_{0}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\theta_{0}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}).

Then we use the similar argument as in
[
12
]
to estimate the velocity
(v_{0},D_{t}v(0))
, pressure
q_{0}
and surface functions
(\xi_{0},\partial_{t}\xi(0))
to complete the proof.

∎

The Lemma
B.6
gives the initial data for
\varepsilon
- nonlinear system (
1.11
).

Proof of Theorem
B.1
.

The results in Lemma
B.6
imply the estimate

\displaystyle\|v_{0}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|q_{0}\|_{W^{1,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\vartheta_{0}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\xi_{0}\|_{W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}+\|D_{t}v(0)\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\vartheta(0)\|_{H^{1}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{t}q(0)\|_{H^{0}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\xi(0)\|_{H^{3/2-\alpha}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}(\|D_{t}u(0)\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\theta(0)\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\|u_{0}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\theta_{0}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\eta_{0}\|_{W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\eta(0)\|_{H^{3/2-\alpha}}^{2})

for
\delta
sufficiently small.

Then by the Banach’s fixed point theory, given
D_{t}^{2}u(0)
,
\partial_{t}^{2}\theta(0)
and
\partial_{t}^{2}\eta(0)
satisfying the conditions in Theorem
B.1
, there exists a unique
(u_{0},p_{0},\theta_{0},\eta_{0},D_{t}u(0),\partial_{t}p(0),\partial_{t}\theta(0),\partial_{t}\eta(0))
obeying the full initial conditions.
∎

B.3.
Initial Forcing Terms for Contraction

We give the forcing terms in the system of (
B.30
) and (
B.31
).

(B.32)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}^{8}

\displaystyle=k\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\mathcal{A}_{0}^{1}-\mathcal{A}_{0}^{2}}\nabla_{\mathcal{A}_{0}^{1}}\vartheta_{0}^{2}+k\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\mathcal{A}_{0}^{2}}\nabla_{\mathcal{A}_{0}^{1}-\mathcal{A}_{0}^{2}}\vartheta_{0}^{2}+F^{8}(\eta^{1}_{0},u^{1}_{0},\theta^{1}_{0})-F^{8}(\eta^{2}_{0},u^{2}_{0},\theta^{2}_{0}),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}^{9}

\displaystyle=-k[\mathcal{N}^{1}(0)-\mathcal{N}^{2}(0)]\cdot\nabla_{\mathcal{A}_{0}^{1}}\vartheta_{0}^{2}-k\mathcal{N}^{2}(0)\cdot\nabla_{\mathcal{A}_{0}^{1}-\mathcal{A}_{0}^{2}}\vartheta_{0}^{2}-\vartheta_{0}^{2}\cdot[|\mathcal{N}^{1}(0)|-|\mathcal{N}^{2}(0)|],

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}^{8}_{1}
